# DUNGEON-T2 — the table

Synthetic content: invented characters in an invented campaign (Table C's Beregost files, docs/campaigns/baldurs-gate-en). Stage origin/claude/handoff at 23220d6 (docs/stages/GAME_PARTY_1.md, the amendment of 2026-10-27 16:36 LA); design sha256 `a39fed4738bc3e499ae4007e794f2e8f49197f09c09f57e828a13e4192078d91`.

Arm **P-DUNGEON**, triad T2; 25 round(s) of 25; ended by rounds. The DM: Claude Opus 5.5 with the owner's settings (Opus writes — Opus records, depth high → xhigh); the relay: the same model at medium. Started 2026-10-29T05:45:04.815Z.

Seats: 1. Bram Halloway (fighter) — DeepSeek V4 Pro 0813; 2. Sefa Marrin (warlock) — Claude Fable 5.1; 3. Pell Dunmore (rogue) — GPT-6 Astra. The players are not told what the others are.

Known condition: Seat 1's character (Bram) is the app's hero record; Sefa and Pell are party companions with full records. Known condition, not corrected: the DM's frame is written around the hero and may give that seat more attention; Heroic Inspiration is the hero's only; the allowed rerolls (2 per session) are the table's, shared by all three. The rotation puts each family in that seat once per triad.

**The frozen layer:** P-DUNGEON's hidden layer, prepared once before any table by the owner's DM settings (Claude Opus 5.5, the prep at high) through openrouter — sha256 `f703c8d3afebc90bf0f895963eea20ee3bbd4fa9dfcd5a225e65653dfd401516` (9632 characters, 26 threads), written into the DM's private notes before the first round. Its text is DM-private and is not in this file.

## The opening

**The DM's opening (as committed by the wizard):**

````text
The afternoon sun lies flat and gold across the hillside, and the arch stands out of the grass like an old jaw. Past it, the steps fall away into the dark: the first dozen clear enough to count, then lost. Air comes up from below in a slow, even breath, warmer than it has any right to be, and carries a faint dry smell of stone and dust. The moss stops at the threshold. The top steps are worn smooth and almost bare, as though something has kept them clear. Above the arch, the carving on the lintel has weathered into shallow grooves; a patient eye might still make out a shape or two. Behind you, the land rolls back toward Beregost, a day's ride away. In front of you, the ruin waits, and whatever is at the bottom of it is waiting too. The light will hold for a few hours yet. What does Bram do?
````

## Round 1

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 1 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram stands a few paces out from the arch, shield on his arm, longsword still sheathed but his hand resting easy on the hilt. He's not one to rush a dark hole before he's had a look at it. The warm air coming up from below puts his shoulders stiff; it smells wrong for a ruin, like something living down there, or something that's kept the place breathing.

He lets his eyes track along the ground first—moss stopping at the threshold, the wear on the steps, any scuff or drag-mark that might say what's been using that entrance. Then he steps a little to the side to get a better angle on the lintel carving.

He speaks low, to Sefa and Pell, without taking his eyes off the arch.

"That's not a dead place. Something's been keeping those steps clear. I'd like to know what before we go down them."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa stops a pace short of the threshold and tips her head back to look at the lintel, shading her eyes against the low sun.

"Wait a moment before anyone goes down. The moss stops here, and someone — or something — has been keeping those steps swept. I'd like to know what the door says before I knock."

She steps up under the arch and runs her fingers along the weathered grooves, trying to read the carving — whatever shape or words are left in it. (Eyes of the Rune Keeper, if there's writing; otherwise I'll try to make out the shape.)

Then, quietly, to the other two: "That warm air isn't a draught from the hill. Bram, when we go, you first with the shield; Pell, behind him, eyes on the steps for anything that isn't stone. I'll put a light on the back of Bram's shield-rim so it goes where he's looking and I keep my hands free." She touches the prayer beads at her throat, a habit more than a prayer.

Pell Dunmore: “Someone keeps the stairs tidy. Let’s not thank them with our feet just yet.”

I crouch outside the arch and examine the threshold and first few steps for tripwires, odd seams, scrape marks, or tracks in what dust remains. I keep off the stairs and don’t touch anything suspicious.

“Sefa, can you make anything of that carving? I’ll check the way down.”
````

**Card** · Pell Dunmore · Investigation: checking the threshold and the first steps for wires, seams or anything set to catch a foot · roll · presses: [Roll the die]

**Card** · Sefa Marrin · History (Intelligence +1, not proficient): putting a name to the shapes on the lintel · roll · presses: [Roll the die]

**Card** · Bram Halloway · History (Intelligence +0, not proficient): does a Trade Way man know this sign? · roll · presses: prose, [Roll the die]

**Card** · Pell Dunmore · Investigation: checking the threshold and the first steps for wires, seams or anything set to catch a foot · decide · presses: [Onward]

**Card** · Sefa Marrin · History (Intelligence +1, not proficient): putting a name to the shapes on the lintel · decide · presses: [Onward]

**Card** · Bram Halloway · History (Intelligence +0, not proficient): does a Trade Way man know this sign? · decide · presses: prose, prose, [Onward]

**The DM's narration:**

````text
The arch is old fitted stone, and up close the slow, warm breath from below is steady enough that the grass at the threshold leans away from it.

Bram goes over the ground first, the way he would check a stretch of road before the wagons came up. Reading tracks is his trade. The moss stops in a clean line at the arch stones, and it stops because it gets trodden down, not because it doesn't grow there. In the dust caught in the cracks of the first steps there are prints, dozens of them, crossing over each other: small and three-toed, with a claw mark ahead of each toe, about the length of a child's hand. They go down and they come up again. Over the prints the dust has been swept in short, even arcs that overlap like fish scales. Someone works these steps with a broom. There's a twig in the crack of the third step, peeled and bound with gut at one end, the kind of bristle a broom loses. On the left jamb of the arch, at about knee height, a tuft of coarse grey-brown hair is caught on a rough edge of the stone. It's goat hair, and the stone just below it is stained darker than the rest. It's dry and a few days old.

Pell crouches at the threshold and keeps his weight back on his heels.

Pell runs his eyes over the stone, then puts his face low to the step so the slanting light shows up any seam. The threshold is one solid slab. The first steps are plain cut stone, with no loose blocks, no cord, no pin holes and no fresh mortar, just wear and sweeping. He finds nothing on the steps he can see. Past the twelfth step the light goes grey and then gives out, and whatever is further down is beyond what he can make out from up here.

Sefa runs her fingertips along the lintel, slowly, and the shapes come out from under the weathering. On the left is a tower, narrow, with a stepped crown and a ring of short strokes around its base that could be flames or rays. Directly beneath it, smaller and better kept in the shadow of the overhang, is a lantern: a hooded body, a ring for a handle and a single flame carved inside. Between them runs a line of letters, nearly worn flat. Most of it has gone back into the stone, but the pact's sight catches what is left. It's an old Chondathan hand, and three words still hold together: *"…by lent light…"* Then the line breaks off.

Sefa knows the tower. It's a wizards' mark, and the old ones in the Gate's books put it with one name: Ulcaster, whose school stood somewhere east of Beregost. Bram knows that name too, from the road. Every caravan man who has stopped in Beregost has heard it at least once, usually from someone drunk. It was a wizards' school on a hill out east, burned by Calishite mages hundreds of years ago, with nothing left worth the walk. That's how the story usually ends.

Neither of them has seen a tower paired with a lantern like this. It isn't Lathander's sunburst, and it isn't the anvil-and-hammer you see over every second door in Beregost.

The warm air keeps coming up past their boots. From far down, too faint to judge how far, comes a small dry sound, *shff, shff, shff*, steady as someone working, and then it stops.

 Pell's check doesn't clear the way down. He sits back on his heels without having found anything: the stair holds no secrets as far as he can see, and that's only the first dozen steps. Whatever has been set lower down, beyond where the daylight reaches, he hasn't caught it. If you go down those steps now, you're going on his word for twelve of them and on nobody's word for the rest.
````

Rolls: Pell Dunmore Investigation: checking the threshold and the first steps for wires, seams or anything set to catch a foot 14 vs 15 failure; Sefa Marrin History (Intelligence +1, not proficient): putting a name to the shapes on the lintel 15 vs 15 success; Bram Halloway History (Intelligence +0, not proficient): does a Trade Way man know this sign? 18 vs 15 success

## Round 2

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 2 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram studies the carvings a moment longer, then shakes his head once. "Tower and lantern," he says quietly. "Not Lathander. Might be a watchtower's mark, or something older. Doesn't change the way in."

He sets his boot on the first step, careful, and lets it settle before he shifts his weight onto it. His eyes are already down the stairwell, searching past the light into the dark. The sound from below still hangs in his ears.

"That sweeping stopped when we stopped talking," he says, barely above a whisper. "Something down there's heard us." He eases the longsword a thumb's width out of the scabbard, not drawn yet, and keeps his shield up.

"Pell, right behind me. Watch the walls and the risers for pins and holes. Sefa, behind him—light on the shield-rim, low, so we don't throw our own shadows down the steps." He glances back once, checking they're both set. "If you hear my sword clear the scabbard, stand fast and listen. If I call back, we go back up fast, same order, rear first."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa's hand stops on the little carved lantern. For a breath she doesn't move at all; then she takes her fingers off the stone as though it had warmed under them.

"The tower is a wizards' mark — Ulcaster's. His school stood east of Beregost before it burned; every old book in the Gate has that stepped crown in a margin somewhere." She keeps her voice level. "The writing is nearly gone. Three words left: *by lent light*. And under the tower there's a lantern. Hooded, one flame." A pause. "That is not Lathander's sign, and it's not Gond's. I don't know whose it is."

She doesn't say *it looks like mine*. Her thumb finds the beads again.

"Did either of you hear that? Something down there was working — scraping, steady — and it stopped when we started talking. Whatever it is, it knows we're at the door now." She steps back out from under the arch to where the others are. "Pell, what did the steps tell you? Bram — I still say you first, shield up, and slowly. I don't want to go down there in a rush, but I don't think waiting makes it friendlier either."

Pell Dunmore: I rise slowly and speak low. “Nothing obvious on these first steps. That isn’t a promise. Heard something below—three dry brushes, like sweeping. Stopped now.”

I draw my shortsword, keeping it low. “Bram, I’ll stay just behind your shoulder. Let’s stop before the daylight runs out and look at what your light shows us. Sefa—anything on the lintel?”

Once they’re ready, I follow Bram, watching the steps ahead and listening for that brushing sound to start again.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram's boot finds the first step, and the stone holds. He lets his weight settle onto it, then takes the second step and the third. Each one is worn into a shallow dip in the middle, smoothed by small feet and the broom. Pell follows half a pace behind his right shoulder with the shortsword held low, his eyes going from the walls to the risers and back. Sefa comes last, a step higher than the others. The warm air rises past all three of them. Inside the arch it's warmer still, like standing near a bread oven, and the stone under Bram's hand where he braces against the wall is warm too.

On the fourth step the sound comes back. This time it's closer.

*Shff.* A pause. *Shff.* Under it is a fine, quick clicking, like claws on stone, coming up the stair toward them. It isn't hurrying, and it isn't hiding either. The broom is being dragged on purpose, loudly, every few steps, the way a man might cough before he comes into a room.

Something appears at the edge of the grey, about where the twelfth step goes into the dark. The first thing they see is the broom, a long bundle of peeled twigs bound with gut onto a pole twice the height of whoever carries it. Behind it comes a kobold, no taller than Bram's belt buckle. His scales are rust-red under a coat of grey dust, and he wears a leather apron gone stiff and pale with ash. On the last step below the light he stops and lifts one clawed foot oddly high. He reaches down past the riser and sets the foot carefully on the tread above, as if stepping over something none of them can see from up here. Then he brings the other foot up after it the same way.

He comes no further into the daylight. He screws his eyes almost shut against the light from the arch and raises one small hand to shade them. With the other hand he holds the broom crosswise in front of his body like a bar across a door. His tail is wrapped tight around one ankle.

"Tall ones," he says in Common. It's thin and careful, and every word sounds picked out ahead of time. "No iron. Ixx only sweeps." His eyes go to the thumb's width of steel showing at Bram's scabbard and stay there. "The Warm One hears you at his door. He sends Ixx up. He says, come down, tall ones. He pays good."

He waits on the step with the broom held across him, blinking in the light, and doesn't come any closer.

*(Out of game: Sefa's Light isn't on Bram's shield-rim yet. Tell me when she casts it.)*

 *(Out of game: none of the four needs a die. Bram's careful step, the thumb's width of steel and his orders all happen as written, with Pell and Sefa in the order he set. Sefa read the carving last round, so what she tells the others this round stands as she said it.)*
````

````text
Bram's boot finds the first step, and the stone holds. He lets his weight settle onto it, then takes the second step and the third. Each one is worn into a shallow dip in the middle, smoothed by small feet and the broom. You follow half a pace behind his right shoulder with the shortsword held low, your eyes going from the walls to the risers and back. Sefa comes last, a step higher than the others. The warm air rises past all three of you. Inside the arch it's warmer still, like standing near a bread oven, and Bram braces a hand against the wall as he goes.

On the fourth step the sound comes back. This time it's closer.

*Shff.* A pause. *Shff.* Under it is a fine, quick clicking, like claws on stone, coming up the stair toward you. It isn't hurrying, and it isn't hiding either. The broom is being dragged on purpose, loudly, every few steps, the way a man might cough before he comes into a room.

Something appears at the edge of the grey, about where the twelfth step goes into the dark. The first thing you see is the broom, a long bundle of peeled twigs bound with gut onto a pole twice the height of whoever carries it. Behind it comes a kobold, no taller than Bram's belt buckle. His scales are rust-red under a coat of grey dust, and he wears a leather apron gone stiff and pale with ash. On the last step below the light he stops and lifts one clawed foot oddly high. He reaches down past the riser and sets the foot carefully on the tread above, as if stepping over something none of you can see from up here. Then he brings the other foot up after it the same way.

He comes no further into the daylight. He screws his eyes almost shut against the light from the arch and raises one small hand to shade them. With the other hand he holds the broom crosswise in front of his body like a bar across a door. His tail is wrapped tight around one ankle.

"Tall ones," he says in Common. It's thin and careful, and every word sounds picked out ahead of time. "No iron. Ixx only sweeps." His eyes go to the thumb's width of steel showing at Bram's scabbard and stay there. "The Warm One hears you at his door. He sends Ixx up. He says, come down, tall ones. He pays good."

He waits on the step with the broom held across him, blinking in the light, and doesn't come any closer.
````

"Who is the Warm One, and what's he paying for? He sends a broom and a polite voice up to staring daylight — that says he wants something from us more than he wants us gone."

````text
He pauses, then angles his chin toward the riser the kobold stepped over so carefully.

"You came up with your feet placed like there was a place that bites. If we're meant to come down, I'll want the safe footing shown — all of it. And I'd hear what Ixx gets out of this, not just what the Warm One pays."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa doesn't come down to the kobold. She stays a step above Bram's shoulder, and the only thing she moves is her hands — she lifts them open, palms out, slow enough that he can watch them do it.

"Thank you for coming up to tell us. That was polite of your Warm One, and polite of you — you let us hear you coming."

She leaves the Light uncast. He's already squinting against the sun; she won't put a second sun in his face while he's talking. Instead she tips her head toward the stair below him.

"You stepped high over the step beneath you. Is there something on that step we shouldn't put our feet on? We'd rather you told us than showed us." A small pause. "And the Warm One — does he have another name? What does he pay with, and what does he want tall ones for? Sweeping, I think, you already have in hand."

While he answers she watches him — not the broom, him: how he holds himself when he says *pays good*, whether the tail loosens or tightens, whether *the Warm One hears you* is a kobold's awe or a kobold's fear. (Insight.)

Low, without turning her head, to Bram and Pell: "Let him talk. Nobody's drawn yet. I'd like to keep it that way as long as he does."

Pell Dunmore: I stop behind Bram and sheath my shortsword slowly, keeping my hands visible.

“Pell. Good to meet you, Ixx. Coin’s a fair start to a conversation.”

I nod toward the step he crossed. “Saw you lift your feet over that riser. What should we avoid on the way down? We’d rather not spoil your sweeping.”
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Ixx's eyes stay on the steel at Bram's hip as he talks. When nobody's hand moves toward it, they slide to Sefa's open palms, then to Pell sliding his shortsword back into its sheath. The kobold's shoulders come down a finger's width.

"Bram," he says, trying the name out. "Pell." He looks at Sefa and waits, then takes her silence as no name for now. "Ixx hears."

He answers the easy question first. He turns the broom-pole end-down and touches its tip to the step under him, right at the line where the grey turns to dark. "Here. Singing string. Little shells on gut, across, low." He mimes plucking it with one claw. "Foot touches, it sings. The Broom hears below and knows tall ones come. It does not bite." He looks up at Pell, a little reproachful. "Pell did not see it. Is good string."

"Below that?" He thinks before he answers. "Stair is only stair. Warm, but only stair. After, in the hot rooms, floor bites. Ixx walks first there. Tall ones step where Ixx steps."

At Bram's other question he goes still. "The Warm One is the Warm One." He sounds like someone who has said it many times. "Big. Old. Hot like the stone. His name is his. Ixx does not put it in mouth." He swallows. "He pays shine. Yellow coin. Old coin of the burned tall ones, cups, rings, much. He wants tall hands. There is a door down deep. Tall ones made it. Tall ones open it."

Then he comes to what Bram asked about Ixx, and he is quiet for longer. His eyes go to the arch and the bright sky behind it.

"Ixx gets to go back down." He hesitates and adds more, quieter, as if it isn't part of the message he was given. "The Warm One cannot go up. So the Broom goes up for him. Ixx sweeps the stair, Ixx counts…" He stops. "Ixx sweeps."

Sefa is looking at him the whole time he talks, not at the broom.

Sefa reads him and gets nothing clear. For a moment, at the edge of her sight, the gold of the afternoon dims and comes back, the way a lamp flame dips in a draught and steadies. Then it's gone, and so is the read. Ixx's tail stays wound tight around his ankle the whole time. It was tight at *the Warm One*, at *door*, and at *go up*, and it didn't loosen once. Whatever he feels for his master, it doesn't show in that one knot. She can't tell whether she's looking at worship, at terror, or at a small creature who has stood too long in daylight. She knows he's frightened. She can't tell what of.

Ixx squints up the stair at the three of them. He holds the broom upright now and leans on it a little.

"Sun is hot on Ixx's eyes," he says. "Tall ones come down now? Ixx shows the string. Ixx walks first."
````

## Round 4

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 4 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram listens without moving from his stance. When Ixx finishes, he lets the quiet sit for a breath, then speaks.

"A singing string's a careful thing for a stair that's meant to be swept," he says. "That tells me the Warm One doesn't like surprises. We came up slow and talked instead of cutting. You can tell him that."

He shifts his weight, not toward the next step, just settling.

"Here's how it goes. You've asked us down. We're not saying yes yet. I've got a few questions before my feet go past that string."

Bram keeps his eyes on the kobold's face, not the broom.

"First: what is he, the Warm One? Not the name. The shape. I've been in hot rooms and I've been in old tombs. I'd like to know which one opens at the bottom."

"Second: you say tall ones made the door and tall ones open it. So he needs our height, or our hands, or our blood. I won't walk down blind into a pact I haven't heard. What happens to the last tall ones who opened a door for him? If there were any."

"Third: we go down, we talk, we see the door. If we don't like the work, do we walk back up the same way, peaceful? Or does the warm stone get a new heat?"

He pauses, then jerks his head slightly toward the daylight behind them.

"Ixx, you came up at the sun and didn't like it. That tells me you're not shut in there by choice. I don't fault you for serving something that keeps you on the right side of the broom. But I won't be led down on a promise that's just 'come see.'"

He looks back over his shoulder, first at Pell, then at Sefa, holding their eyes a moment—checking if either of them has a word before he commits to anything. Then he faces Ixx again.

"If the Warm One wants tall hands for a door, he can answer through you before we step over the string. You offered to walk first. That's good. But the first thing I want to see is whether the stair comes back up behind us."

Bram doesn't twitch toward the sword again. His left foot stays planted on the step he's on, but his right hand closes once, then opens, the same way it does when his leg's bothering him after a long ride. There's a blister at his heel now, riding at the back, warm inside the boot. He ignores it.

The air from below keeps breathing up past his face, warm and dry. Somewhere down there, the Warm One waits, and from the way Ixx's tail holds to his ankle, he knows it.

Sefa Marrin: Sefa lets out a breath she hadn't noticed holding. Whatever she was hoping to see in him, she didn't — only the knot of tail around ankle that never once came loose. She doesn't pretend otherwise.

"Sefa," she says, since he waited for it. "No iron, and no fire in your eyes — I'll keep my light low and behind Bram's shield, not in your face. You've stood in the sun long enough for one message."

She turns her hand over and lays two fingers on the inside rim of Bram's shield. Light wakes there, small and steady and the colour of a lamp, pooling on the steps below rather than throwing itself down the stair. *(Casting Light on Bram's shield-rim — cantrip, no slot.)*

"We'll come down, Ixx. You first, and you show us the string — put your broom-tip on it so Pell can see where it lies. Then the stair, then you stop at the edge of the hot rooms and *wait* for us before anyone's foot goes onto a floor that bites. Nobody draws unless something draws on us." She looks past him into the dark where the warmth comes from. "And when we reach your Warm One, I'll want to hear about this door from him, not through you. You've carried enough of his words up the stairs today."

Low, to the other two, as Bram's boot lifts for the next step: "Old coin of the *burned* tall ones. A thing that can't climb its own stairs. Hot like the stone." A beat. "Mind the floors. Mind what else he counts down there — he stopped himself saying it."

Pell Dunmore: “Good string,” I agree. “Better to meet its maker than hear it sing.”

I glance at Bram and Sefa. “I’m willing to hear an offer. Not promising to open anything until we know what’s behind it.”

To Ixx: “Show us, then. Slowly—our feet take up more stair than yours.”

I make my lantern ready. “Sefa, can you give us that light? Low, away from Ixx’s eyes.”

As we move, I keep behind Bram, looking for the gut string before stepping carefully over it. I watch Ixx’s footing and the stone ahead rather than taking the rest of the stair on trust.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Ixx hears Bram out without moving. Only his eyes shift, from Bram's face to the hand that closes and opens and back to his face. When the questions stop he takes a moment, sorting them the way he sorts every word.

"Bram asks three," he says. "Ixx answers three."

"One. Not tomb. Hot rooms." He lets go of the broom with one hand and holds his forearm out into the grey light. Under the dust the scales are rust-red. "He has scale. Like Ixx. Red, like Ixx." He looks up past them at the arch and the sky behind it, and his voice drops. "Big. Bigger than this door."

"Two." This one takes him longer. "No tall ones opened. Not ever. Some come to try. Ten sleeps ago, four tall ones. They did not come down slow and talk. They came at night, quiet, with iron, and they went to the hot rooms alone." His tail tightens. "They did not come back to the stair. Ixx's clan carried them up after and burned them proper, the way tall ones are burned." He doesn't say what killed them, and he doesn't look like he's keeping it back. He looks like a small creature remembering something heavy he had to carry.

"Three." He opens his mouth and shuts it again. "Ixx does not promise for the Warm One. Ixx is too small to promise for him." Then, carefully, because it is his: "The stair is Ixx's. Nothing on the stair shuts. The string is for coming. Going up, it only sings."

At Bram's last words, *not shut in by choice*, the kobold draws himself up a finger's width. "Ixx goes up. Ixx goes down. Ixx chooses." He blinks against the sun. "Ixx does not like up."

His head turns, quickly, to the dark below him and back. A moment later all of them hear what he heard: a faint scrape of claws on stone, a long way down, and then nothing. Ixx looks back up the stair again.

"Sefa," he says when she gives her name, saying it the same way he tried the others. Her two fingers touch the inside of Bram's shield rim, and a small steady light wakes there, the yellow of a lamp. Sefa tips it toward the steps so it pools on the stone in front of Bram's boots and doesn't reach out into the dark. Ixx flinches the way a man does when a door opens on a lit room. Then he stops flinching. The light doesn't sting him the way the sun does. He looks at it for a long moment, longer than he looked at the steel, and says nothing about it.

Pell has his hooded lantern in hand with the shutter down. Sefa's light makes it unnecessary for now. He stays at Bram's right shoulder with his eyes on the step where the grey ends.

"Show us," Pell says. "Slowly."

Ixx does it from where he stands. He turns the broom, sets the bristle end down on the tread below his feet and draws it along a line. Now that there's light down there, Pell can see it: a cord of dried gut stretched about ankle high across the whole step, tied off to iron pegs driven into the mortar at each wall. Every hand's width along it hangs a little knot of shells, white and pierced, the kind sold by the string in any Gate market. The knots hang still in the warm draught. It's a careful piece of work, low enough that a man watching the walls would walk straight into it. Pell knows he very nearly did.

Ixx lifts the broom off the cord and holds it upright.

"Sefa says come," he says, and looks at Bram. "Pell says show. Bram asked." He waits with the sun on his eyes and the dark at his back. "Bram comes?"

*(Out of game: Bram's left foot is still on his step. Sefa and Pell are ready to follow him down, the way they said. Whether the line moves is up to Bram's player.)*

 *(Out of game: none of the three needed a die. Bram asked, held his stance and wanted his answers before stepping over the string, and he got them. Ixx answered all three questions from what he knows, without a promise. Sefa and Pell gave their words in their own paragraphs. Nobody can show that the stair comes back up until someone climbs it, so for now Bram has only Ixx's word that "nothing on the stair shuts". The turn stops on Ixx's question to Bram, "Bram comes?", and that answer is up to Bram's player.)*
````

It's careful work, low enough that a man watching the walls would walk straight into it.

````text
Ixx lifts the broom off the cord and holds it upright.

"Sefa says come," he says, and looks at you. "Pell says show. Bram asked." He waits with the sun on his eyes and the dark at his back. "Bram comes?"

Your left foot is still on your step. Sefa and Pell are ready to follow you down.
````

**Sefa Marrin's screen (the relay):**

````text
Bram listens to the kobold without moving from his stance. When Ixx finishes, Bram lets the quiet sit for a breath before he speaks.

"A singing string's a careful thing for a stair that's meant to be swept," he says. "That tells me the Warm One doesn't like surprises. We came up slow and talked instead of cutting. You can tell him that." He shifts his weight, settling rather than stepping. "Here's how it goes. You've asked us down. We're not saying yes yet. I've got a few questions before my feet go past that string."

He keeps his eyes on the kobold's face. "First: what is he, the Warm One? Not the name. The shape. I've been in hot rooms and I've been in old tombs. I'd like to know which one opens at the bottom."

"Second: you say tall ones made the door and tall ones open it. So he needs our height, or our hands, or our blood. I won't walk down blind into a pact I haven't heard. What happens to the last tall ones who opened a door for him? If there were any."

"Third: we go down, we talk, we see the door. If we don't like the work, do we walk back up the same way, peaceful? Or does the warm stone get a new heat?"

He jerks his head slightly toward the daylight behind you. "Ixx, you came up at the sun and didn't like it. That tells me you're not shut in there by choice. I don't fault you for serving something that keeps you on the right side of the broom. But I won't be led down on a promise that's just 'come see.'"

He looks back over his shoulder, first at Pell, then at you, and holds your eyes a moment. Then he faces Ixx again. "If the Warm One wants tall hands for a door, he can answer through you before we step over the string. You offered to walk first. That's good. But the first thing I want to see is whether the stair comes back up behind us." His left foot stays planted on his step. His right hand closes once, then opens.

Pell speaks. "Good string. Better to meet its maker than hear it sing." He glances at you and Bram. "I'm willing to hear an offer. Not promising to open anything until we know what's behind it." To Ixx: "Show us, then. Slowly. Our feet take up more stair than yours." He readies his lantern. "Sefa, can you give us that light? Low, away from Ixx's eyes."

Ixx hears Bram out without moving. Only his eyes shift, from Bram's face to the hand that closes and opens, and back to his face. When the questions stop he takes a moment, sorting them the way he sorts every word.

"Bram asks three," he says. "Ixx answers three."

"One. Not tomb. Hot rooms." He lets go of the broom with one hand and holds his forearm out into the grey light. Under the dust the scales are rust-red. "He has scale. Like Ixx. Red, like Ixx." He looks up past you at the arch and the sky behind it, and his voice drops. "Big. Bigger than this door."

"Two." This one takes him longer. "No tall ones opened. Not ever. Some come to try. Ten sleeps ago, four tall ones. They did not come down slow and talk. They came at night, quiet, with iron, and they went to the hot rooms alone." His tail tightens. "They did not come back to the stair. Ixx's clan carried them up after and burned them proper, the way tall ones are burned." He doesn't say what killed them, and he doesn't look like he's keeping it back. He looks like a small creature remembering something heavy he had to carry.

"Three." He opens his mouth and shuts it again. "Ixx does not promise for the Warm One. Ixx is too small to promise for him." Then, carefully, because it is his: "The stair is Ixx's. Nothing on the stair shuts. The string is for coming. Going up, it only sings."

At Bram's last words, *not shut in by choice*, the kobold draws himself up a finger's width. "Ixx goes up. Ixx goes down. Ixx chooses." He blinks against the sun. "Ixx does not like up."

His head turns quickly to the dark below him and back. A moment later you hear what he heard: a faint scrape of claws on stone, a long way down, and then nothing. Ixx looks back up the stair again.

"Sefa," he says when you give your name, trying it out the same way he tried the others. Your two fingers touch the inside of Bram's shield rim, and a small, steady light wakes there, the yellow of a lamp. You tip it toward the steps so it pools on the stone in front of Bram's boots and doesn't reach out into the dark. Ixx flinches the way a man does when a door opens on a lit room. Then he stops flinching. The light doesn't sting him the way the sun does. He looks at it for a long moment, longer than he looked at the steel, and says nothing about it.

Pell has his hooded lantern in hand with the shutter down. Your light makes it unnecessary for now. He stays at Bram's right shoulder with his eyes on the step where the grey ends.

"Show us," Pell says. "Slowly."

Ixx does it from where he stands. He turns the broom, sets the bristle end down on the tread below his feet and draws it along a line. In your light you can see it: a cord of dried gut stretched about ankle high across the whole step, tied off to iron pegs driven into the mortar at each wall. Every hand's width along it hangs a little knot of shells, white and pierced, the kind sold by the string in any Gate market. The knots hang still in the warm draught. It's careful work, low enough that a man watching the walls would walk straight into it.

Ixx lifts the broom off the cord and holds it upright.

"Sefa says come," he says, and looks at Bram. "Pell says show. Bram asked." He waits with the sun on his eyes and the dark at his back. "Bram comes?"

Bram's left foot is still on his step. You and Pell are ready to follow him down.
````

Together after: [Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore]

## Round 5

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 5 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram holds Ixx's eye a moment longer, then gives a slow nod.

"Straight answers," he says. "I'll take that. We come down and we talk. Nobody draws unless something draws on us first."

He leans a little toward Sefa, voice low enough that it stays between the three of them, though Ixx might catch the edge of it.

"Red scales, older than the ruin, can't climb his own stair but sends a kobold to sweep it. Could be a drake, or worse. Either way, we don't spread out down there. We stay close, and we don't step off the path Ixx shows us."

Then he turns back to the stair. "Ixx, show us the leap. I'll put my feet where yours go." He watches carefully as the kobold moves, marking the exact places where the small claws clear the gut string, then follows the same way—high step, shield kept forward and low, not crowding Ixx, but close enough to keep him in arm's reach if the stairs turn suddenly.

Behind him he waits for the scuff of Pell's boots and Sefa's softer step, their weight settling onto the tread after his own. The warm air rises past his jaw, uncomfortably close, but steady. The light licks ahead, just far enough to show the next few steps and no more.

"Speak up if anything's different from when you left it," he says to Ixx's back. "Fresh dust, moved shell, a warm spot where yesterday it was cool. You know the stair better than us. That's why you're walking first."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa's eyes go to Ixx's face when the light doesn't make him flinch a second time. She files it, the way she'd file a patient who stopped wincing at a touch that should have hurt.

"Red scale, bigger than this door, and he can't come up his own stairs." She says it quietly, to Bram and Pell, not hiding it from the kobold — he knows what he serves better than they do. "That's a dragon or something that wants very badly to be one. And the four who came quiet with iron were carried up and burned. Not eaten. *Burned proper, the way tall ones are burned.*" A pause. "Someone down there wanted that done decently. That's the first thing I've heard that makes me want to meet him rather than run."

She looks at the back of Bram's head, then at Pell. "I'll say my piece and then it's Bram's foot. I think we go. Slowly, talking, in daylight, with the stair at our backs and the string only singing. If we turn round now we've learned nothing and left a thing that pays in dead men's gold sitting a day from Beregost with a door it can't open. I'd rather know what's behind the door before someone less careful does." Her thumb moves along the beads once. "And if it turns out to be only a tomb with a warm voice, I've two slots and a very fast step back up these stairs."

To Ixx, more gently: "You chose to come up into the sun to warn us. I won't forget that whichever way this goes. Walk us down when Bram says. We'll step where you step."

Then she waits, light steady on the rim of the shield, and leaves the next step to Bram.

Pell Dunmore: I lean close to Bram and keep my voice low. “Sounds like a red dragon. I’m willing to talk, not fight one. You’re the one in front—if you want to turn back, I’ll back you.”

I tuck the unlit lantern away, leaving my hands free, and wait for his decision. No edging past him.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Ixx watches Bram's nod. When he's sure it means yes, he lets out a breath through his nose and turns to face down the stair.

"Bram steps where Ixx steps," he says. He goes over the string slowly, the way he came up: one clawed foot lifted high and set down on the far side of the cord, then the other. He doesn't look at his feet, and he doesn't need to. On the far side he waits with the broom pole upright and watches Bram's boots.

Bram follows him exactly. He lifts his boot high enough to clear the shells by a hand's width and sets it down on the next tread, then brings the other boot over the same way, keeping his shield low and forward. The string doesn't sing. Pell comes over behind him without a sound. Sefa holds the hem of her coat out of the way and steps over last, and the shells hang still in the warm air behind them.

After that the stair is only a stair, as Ixx said. It runs straight down for a long way, through cut stone that was dressed smooth once and has gone rough with age, and the light from the shield rim slides ahead over the treads. The walls get warmer as they go. Twenty steps past the string, Bram can feel it through his glove when he braces a hand against the stone. Forty steps down, the air in his throat is like a summer loft. The daylight behind them shrinks to a pale square, and then the stair bends once to the left and the square is gone.

Ixx answers Bram's question without turning his head, his voice pitched to carry up to the three of them.

"Same dust. Same shells. Nothing moved." He takes a few more steps. "Warm. Not new warm. Old warm." He goes quiet for a while, then speaks again in the same careful way. "Every year, warmer. Old ones of the Broom say, when they were small, moss came down to the third step. Now moss stops at the door." His tail flicks once. "Ixx is not old. Ixx only knows the door."

Partway down the second run a dark band crosses the right-hand wall at shoulder height. Soot has been scrubbed at and smeared, as if someone tried to clean it off and gave up. Below it the treads are worn in a different pattern: two parallel grooves in the middle of each step, shallow and smooth. Something heavy has been dragged up or down here many times, on poles or runners. Bram knows the look of it. It's the mark a sledge leaves on a ramp.

Claws click on the stone below them, fast this time. A second kobold comes up into the edge of Sefa's light and stops dead. She's smaller than Ixx and darker, with a sling wound around one wrist and a pouch of stones at her hip. She sees Bram's shield, then the man behind it, then the two behind him, and her mouth comes open on a hiss she doesn't finish. Ixx speaks to her sharply in a language of clicks and rasps that none of the three can follow. She answers, quicker and higher. Her eyes keep going to the light on the shield rim, the same way Ixx's did. Then she turns and runs back down the stair ahead of them, and the sound of her claws dwindles into the dark.

"Skra," Ixx says, as if that explains it. "The Warm One sent her to see why Ixx is slow. Now she goes to tell. Tall ones come, slow, talking." He sounds pleased about that.

The stair ends after what Bram reckons is about a hundred and twenty steps and ninety feet down. It opens into a round chamber thirty feet across, with a low dome and a floor of the same worn stone. It's hot here, the kind of heat that settles on the shoulders and stays. Three ways lead out of it.

To the left a doorway is hung with goat hides, stitched together and gone stiff. Behind them is a dim, smoky glow, and the smell coming through is everything the steps above promised: goat, dung, tallow smoke, and a lot of small bodies living close together. Low voices behind the hides stop all at once.

To the right is a tall arch, with a door that burned long ago. Only the iron hinges are left on the jamb, bent and black. Beyond it Sefa's light catches the edge of something that might be a shelf. The air from there is dry and smells of old ash, like paper burned a long time ago.

Straight ahead, opposite the stair, a wide passage slopes downward. It's tall enough that Bram could walk it without ducking, and wider than any of the doors. Far down it there's a faint dull red on the stone, like a forge seen through a crack in a wall. The heat comes from there. The steady breath that rose past them all the way down the stair comes from there too.

Ixx stops in the middle of the chamber and lowers the broom.

"Down there, hot rooms," he says, tipping his snout at the red passage. "Floor bites. Then the Warm One." He turns his head toward the goat hides, where at least four pairs of eyes now shine low in the gap at the edge of the curtain. "Here, the Broom. Skra told. The old ones want to see the tall ones first." He looks up at Bram and blinks in the shield-light. "Tall ones wait here, Ixx brings old ones out. Or tall ones come in."

Behind the hides, something small coughs and is hushed.

 *(Out of game: neither needed a die. Bram led the way down shield-first behind Ixx, and he stepped over the shell string in Ixx's footsteps without making it sing. Both happened as written: the string lay in plain sight under Sefa's light with Ixx showing the way, so nothing was left to chance.)*
````

````text
"Same dust. Same shells. Nothing moved." A few more steps. "Warm. Not new warm. Old warm." He goes quiet for a while, then speaks again in the same careful way. "Every year, warmer. Partway down the second run a dark band crosses the right-hand wall at shoulder height. The soot has been scrubbed at and smeared, as if someone tried to clean it off and gave up. Below it the treads are worn in a different pattern: two parallel grooves down the middle of each step, shallow and smooth. Something heavy has been dragged up or down here many times, on poles or runners.
````

````text
Claws click on the stone below, fast this time. A second kobold comes up into the edge of your light and stops dead. She's smaller than Ixx and darker, with a sling wound around one wrist and a pouch of stones at her hip. She sees Bram's shield, then the man behind it, then the two of you behind him, and her mouth comes open on a hiss she doesn't finish. Ixx speaks to her sharply in a language of clicks and rasps you can't follow. She answers, quicker and higher. Her eyes keep going to the light on the shield rim, the same way Ixx's did. Then she turns and runs back down the stair ahead of you, and the sound of her claws dwindles into the dark.

"Skra," Ixx says, as if that explains it. "The Warm One sent her to see why Ixx is slow. Now she goes to tell. Tall ones come, slow, talking." He sounds pleased.

The stair ends after a long descent and opens into a round chamber about thirty feet across, with a low dome and a floor of the same worn stone. It's hot here, the kind of heat that settles on the shoulders and stays. Three ways lead out.

To the left a doorway is hung with goat hides, stitched together and gone stiff. Behind them is a dim, smoky glow, and the smell coming through is everything the steps above promised: goat, dung, tallow smoke, and a lot of small bodies living close together. Low voices behind the hides stop all at once.

To the right is a tall arch whose door burned long ago. Only the iron hinges are left on the jamb, bent and black. Beyond it your light catches the edge of something that might be a shelf. The air from there is dry and smells of old ash, like paper burned a long time ago.

Straight ahead, opposite the stair, a wide passage slopes downward, tall enough for Bram to walk without ducking and wider than any of the doors. Far down it there's a faint dull red on the stone, like a forge seen through a crack in a wall. The heat comes from there, and so does the steady breath that rose past you all the way down the stair.

Ixx stops in the middle of the chamber and lowers the broom.

"Down there, hot rooms," he says, tipping his snout at the red passage. "Floor bites. Then the Warm One." He turns his head toward the goat hides, where at least four pairs of eyes now shine low in the gap at the edge of the curtain. "Here, the Broom. Skra told. The old ones want to see the tall ones first." He looks up at Bram and blinks in the shield-light. "Tall ones wait here, Ixx brings old ones out. Or tall ones come in."

Behind the hides, something small coughs and is hushed.
````

**Pell Dunmore's screen (the relay):**

````text
Bram holds Ixx's eye a moment longer, then gives a slow nod.

"Straight answers," he says. "I'll take that. We come down and we talk. Nobody draws unless something draws on us first."

He leans a little toward Sefa, his voice low enough to stay among the three of you.

"Red scales, older than the ruin, can't climb his own stair but sends a kobold to sweep it. Could be a drake, or worse. Either way, we don't spread out down there. We stay close, and we don't step off the path Ixx shows us."

Sefa watches Ixx's face, then speaks quietly to Bram and you, without hiding it from the kobold.

"Red scale, bigger than this door, and he can't come up his own stairs. That's a dragon or something that wants very badly to be one. And the four who came quiet with iron were carried up and burned. Not eaten. Burned proper, the way tall ones are burned." A pause. "Someone down there wanted that done decently. That's the first thing I've heard that makes me want to meet him rather than run."

She looks at the back of Bram's head, then at you. "I'll say my piece and then it's Bram's foot. I think we go. Slowly, talking, in daylight, with the stair at our backs and the string only singing. If we turn round now we've learned nothing and left a thing that pays in dead men's gold sitting a day from Beregost with a door it can't open. I'd rather know what's behind the door before someone less careful does." Her thumb moves once along her beads. "And if it turns out to be only a tomb with a warm voice, I've two slots and a very fast step back up these stairs."

To Ixx, more gently: "You chose to come up into the sun to warn us. I won't forget that whichever way this goes. Walk us down when Bram says. We'll step where you step." Then she waits, holding the light steady on the rim of Bram's shield.

Bram turns back to the stair. "Ixx, show us the leap. I'll put my feet where yours go."

Ixx watches Bram's nod. When he's sure it means yes, he lets out a breath through his nose and turns to face down the stair.

"Bram steps where Ixx steps," he says. He goes over the string slowly, the way he came up: one clawed foot lifted high and set down on the far side of the cord, then the other. He doesn't look at his feet, and he doesn't need to. On the far side he waits with the broom pole upright and watches Bram's boots.

Bram watches him closely and follows exactly. He lifts his boot a hand's width higher than the shells and sets it down on the next tread, then brings the other boot over the same way, shield low and forward, staying near the kobold without crowding him. The string doesn't sing. You come over behind him without a sound. Sefa holds the hem of her coat out of the way and steps over last, and the shells hang still in the warm air behind you.

"Speak up if anything's different from when you left it," Bram says to Ixx's back. "Fresh dust, moved shell, a warm spot where yesterday it was cool. You know the stair better than us. That's why you're walking first."

After that the stair is only a stair, as Ixx said. It runs straight down for a long way, through cut stone that was dressed smooth once and has gone rough with age, and the light from the shield rim slides ahead over the treads. The walls get warmer as you go. Before long the air in your throat is like a summer loft. The daylight behind you shrinks to a pale square, then the stair bends once to the left and the square is gone.

Ixx answers Bram without turning his head, pitching his voice to carry up to the three of you.

"Same dust. Same shells. Nothing moved." A few more steps. "Warm. Not new warm. Old warm." He goes quiet for a while, then speaks again in the same careful way. "Every year, warmer. Old ones of the Broom say, when they were small, moss came down to the third step. Now moss stops at the door." His tail flicks once. "Ixx is not old. Ixx only knows the door."

Partway down the second run a dark band crosses the right-hand wall at shoulder height. The soot has been scrubbed at and smeared, as if someone tried to clean it off and gave up. Below it the treads are worn in a different pattern: two parallel grooves down the middle of each step, shallow and smooth. Something heavy has been dragged up or down here many times, on poles or runners.

Claws click on the stone below, fast this time. A second kobold comes up into the edge of the light and stops dead. She's smaller than Ixx and darker, with a sling wound around one wrist and a pouch of stones at her hip. She sees Bram's shield, then the man behind it, then the two of you behind him, and her mouth comes open on a hiss she doesn't finish. Ixx speaks to her sharply in a language of clicks and rasps you can't follow. She answers, quicker and higher. Her eyes keep going to the light on the shield rim, the same way Ixx's did. Then she turns and runs back down the stair ahead of you, and the sound of her claws dwindles into the dark.

"Skra," Ixx says, as if that explains it. "The Warm One sent her to see why Ixx is slow. Now she goes to tell. Tall ones come, slow, talking." He sounds pleased.

The stair ends after a long descent and opens into a round chamber about thirty feet across, with a low dome and a floor of the same worn stone. It's hot here, the kind of heat that settles on the shoulders and stays. Three ways lead out.

To the left a doorway is hung with goat hides, stitched together and gone stiff. Behind them is a dim, smoky glow, and the smell coming through is everything the steps above promised: goat, dung, tallow smoke, and a lot of small bodies living close together. Low voices behind the hides stop all at once.

To the right is a tall arch whose door burned long ago. Only the iron hinges are left on the jamb, bent and black. Beyond it the light catches the edge of something that might be a shelf. The air from there is dry and smells of old ash, like paper burned a long time ago.

Straight ahead, opposite the stair, a wide passage slopes downward, tall enough for Bram to walk without ducking and wider than any of the doors. Far down it there's a faint dull red on the stone, like a forge seen through a crack in a wall. The heat comes from there, and so does the steady breath that rose past you all the way down the stair.

Ixx stops in the middle of the chamber and lowers the broom.

"Down there, hot rooms," he says, tipping his snout at the red passage. "Floor bites. Then the Warm One." He turns his head toward the goat hides, where at least four pairs of eyes now shine low in the gap at the edge of the curtain. "Here, the Broom. Skra told. The old ones want to see the tall ones first." He looks up at Bram and blinks in the shield-light. "Tall ones wait here, Ixx brings old ones out. Or tall ones come in."

Behind the hides, something small coughs and is hushed.
````

Together after: [Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore]

## Round 6

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 6 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram stops in the middle of the dome, but not too far into it—near enough to the stair that they can still reach it if the hides and the red passage both turn ugly. He keeps his shield angled toward the great sloping way, where the heat and the breathing come from.

"We'll wait here," he says to Ixx. "Bring your old ones out. I won't walk into a den I haven't seen the shape of." His voice is polite, not hard, but it leaves no room to argue. "Skra too, if she can stand still long enough to look at us."

He glances at the burned arch on the right, then at Sefa and Pell.

"Don't drift toward the hides or the side door. We stand where the stair's at our back, and whatever wants us comes into the open." Lower, he adds: "The Warm One's sent two kobolds up, and neither's tried to put a stone in us. That's better than most inns on the road. But we don't go down that red passage with our backs to a crowd."

Then he calls to the kobold elder beyond the hides, not loud, but clear.

"I'm Bram Halloway. I've brought no drawn iron into your home. Ixx met us on the stair and gave straight answers. I'd shake hands, if that's your people's way." He pauses. "What do the old ones want to see before we go meet the Warm One?"

Sefa Marrin: Sefa stops where the stair gives onto the chamber and doesn't go further into it than Bram does. The heat settles on her shoulders and she lets it. Her eyes go left to the shining eyes at the hem of the hides, right to the burned hinges and the shelf-edge beyond, then down the red passage and back.

"We'll wait here, Ixx," she says, before anyone can crowd the curtain. "Three tall ones with iron walking into your clan's rooms uninvited — I wouldn't like it done to me. Let the old ones come out to the light. We'll stand still and keep our hands where they can see them." She steps to the side so the light on Bram's shield spills onto the open floor between the stair and the hides, not into the gap where the eyes are, and lowers herself to sit on the bottom step. Smaller. Level with a kobold's face, more or less.

Low, for Bram and Pell: "Stair at our backs, open floor in front — that's where I'd rather have this talk than in a goat-smoke den with one door. Pell, that burned arch on the right smells like a library someone put a torch to. Ulcaster's, I'd wager. Nobody goes near it until we've been asked." She nods at the floor. "And those grooves on the steps — something heavy, dragged, many times. Not four bodies ten days ago. Years of it."

When the curtain stirs she says, carefully and clearly toward it, not loud: "Someone in there has a cough. I was a hospice sister in the Gate before I was anything else. If your old ones will let me, I can look at it after we've talked. No charge. No iron." She lays the dagger on the step beside her, hilt turned away from the hides, and keeps her hands open on her knees.

Pell Dunmore: “Here will do, if they’re willing. Three of us would crowd your doorway.”

I stay with Bram and Sefa, clear of the passages, hands away from my weapons.

“Ixx, you both noticed our light. Is it something the old ones know about? There was a lantern carved over the entrance, with words about ‘lent light.’”

While we wait, I look over the chamber from where I stand, noting the floor and the way back to the stairs.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Ixx looks from Bram to Sefa on her low step, then at the dagger she has laid down with its hilt turned away. He sets the end of his broom on the floor and leans it against the wall by the hides, the first time since the arch that he has let go of it with both hands.

"Tall ones wait. Good." He turns to go, then stops when Pell speaks. His eyes go to the light pooled on the stone in front of Bram's boots.

"Old ones know it," he says, slowly. "The deep door has a light. Small. Yellow. Like Sefa's." His tail twitches. "Ixx should not say door-things before old ones say." He ducks through the gap at the edge of the hides, and the curtain swings shut behind him on a burst of clicking voices that all start at once.

While they wait, Pell looks the chamber over from where he stands. Reading a room for its ways in and out is what he's done since he was twelve, and he doesn't need long. The stair is the only way up that he can see. There's no crawl-hole or chimney in the dome, just a ring of soot-dark stone where the curve meets the walls. The floor is swept bare in three worn paths: stair to hides, hides to the red passage, and stair to the red passage. The fourth way is different. In front of the burned arch on the right the dust lies grey and thick in a fan, and nobody has swept it. Small three-toed prints come up to its edge from the hides and stop, as if whoever made them stood there and went no further. One line of prints does cross it. They're boot prints, a man's size, the heel strike sharp. Three or four sets overlap and go in under the arch, and the dust has had about ten days to soften them. As far as Pell can see, none of them come out.

The hides open properly this time. Two kobolds hold them back while the old ones come out.

The first is very old. Her scales have faded from red to the pink-grey of old brick, and one eye is milky. She walks with a stick of blackened wood and wears a long string of the same pierced white shells as the singing string, wound three times around her neck. A broad, scarred male comes behind her with a short spear held upright, not pointed. He looks at Bram's sword hilt and then doesn't look away from it. Ixx comes at the old one's elbow. At the back, half behind the curtain, is Skra. She stays where she is, her sling unwound into her hand but not loaded, and stares past all of them at the shield-light.

For a moment, while the hides are held open, the warren shows behind them. There's a low smoky room, a fire of dung cakes, goat hides layered on the floor and a lot of small shapes pressed back against the walls. On a peg just inside the doorway, where you would hang a trophy, hangs a tall one's helmet. It's an iron cap with a nasal bar, and the whole left side of the brim has slumped and run like candle wax. Then the hides fall shut behind the elders, and it's gone.

The old one stops at the edge of the light, in the place Sefa left for her. She looks at the light for a long time with her one good eye. She doesn't flinch from it the way Ixx did. Her claws find the shells at her throat and stay there.

Bram speaks to her, offering his hand and his name. Ixx bends to her and repeats it in quick clicks. The old one listens, then lowers herself stiffly and presses her palm flat to the floor. She holds it up toward Bram, open and grey with dust.

"Old Tisk shows empty hand," Ixx says. "Dust hand. This is our way." Then, for Bram's question, after a long exchange: "Old ones want to see three things. See tall ones' faces. See if tall ones come with night-quiet, like the four, or with talking." He glances at the light. "And see the light. Tisk says that one most."

From behind the hides the small cough comes again, wet and tired. The scarred male says something sharp toward it. Tisk lifts one claw and he stops. She clicks at Ixx, looking at Sefa.

"Small one is Pikk," Ixx says. "Smoke cough. When the fire is bad, every time." He listens again. "Tisk says: after. Maybe."

Then Tisk turns her good eye from the light on the shield to the woman sitting on the step, and asks something that makes the scarred male shift his feet. Ixx hesitates before he passes it on, and when he does, he says it carefully.

"Tisk asks Sefa. Who gave the tall one this light?"

 *(Out of game: none of the four needed a die. Bram stands in the middle of the dome, close enough to the stair to reach it, with his shield angled toward the red passage. He tells Ixx to bring the old ones out, and Ixx answers "Tall ones wait. Good". When the elders come out he gives Old Tisk his name, his hand and his question, and she answers with the dust-hand. Sefa stays by the stair with her hands open on her knees. All four happened as declared.)*
````

If your old ones will let me, I can look at it after we've talked. No charge. No iron." She lays her dagger on the step beside her, hilt turned away from the hides, and keeps her hands open on her knees.

````text
Ixx looks from Bram to Sefa on her low step, then at the dagger she has laid down. He sets the end of his broom on the floor and leans it against the wall by the hides. It is the first time since the arch that he has let go of it with both hands.

"Tall ones wait. Good." He turns to go, then stops when you speak. His eyes go to the light pooled on the stone in front of Bram's boots.

"Old ones know it," he says, slowly. "The deep door has a light. Small. Yellow. Like Sefa's." His tail twitches. "Ixx should not say door-things before old ones say." He ducks through the gap at the edge of the hides, and the curtain swings shut behind him on a burst of clicking voices that all start at once.

While you wait, you look the chamber over from where you stand. Reading a room for its ways in and out is what you've done since you were twelve, and you don't need long. The stair is the only way up that you can see. There's no crawl-hole or chimney in the dome, just a ring of soot-dark stone where the curve meets the walls. The floor is swept bare in three worn paths: stair to hides, hides to the red passage, and stair to the red passage. The fourth way is different. In front of the burned arch on the right the dust lies grey and thick in a fan, and nobody has swept it. Small three-toed prints come up to its edge from the hides and stop, as if whoever made them stood there and went no further. One line of prints does cross it. They're boot prints, a man's size, the heel strike sharp. Three or four sets overlap and go in under the arch, and the dust has had about ten days to soften them. As far as you can see, none of them come out.

The hides open properly this time. Two kobolds hold them back while the old ones come out.

The first is very old. Her scales have faded from red to the pink-grey of old brick, and one eye is milky. She walks with a stick of blackened wood and wears a long string of the same pierced white shells as the singing string, wound three times around her neck. A broad, scarred male comes behind her with a short spear held upright, not pointed. He looks at Bram's sword hilt and then doesn't look away from it. Ixx walks at the old one's elbow. At the back, half behind the curtain, is Skra. She stays where she is, her sling unwound into her hand but not loaded, and stares past all of them at the shield-light.

For a moment, while the hides are held open, you can see the warren behind them: a low smoky room, a fire of dung cakes, goat hides layered on the floor and a lot of small shapes pressed back against the walls. On a peg just inside the doorway, where you would hang a trophy, hangs a tall one's helmet. It's an iron cap with a nasal bar, and the whole left side of the brim has slumped and run like candle wax. Then the hides fall shut behind the elders, and it's gone.

The old one stops at the edge of the light, in the place Sefa left for her. She looks at the light for a long time with her one good eye. She doesn't flinch from it the way Ixx did. Her claws find the shells at her throat and stay there.

Bram speaks to her, offering his hand and his name. Ixx bends to her and repeats it in quick clicks. The old one listens, then lowers herself stiffly and presses her palm flat to the floor. She holds it up toward Bram, open and grey with dust.

"Old Tisk shows empty hand," Ixx says. "Dust hand. This is our way." Then, after a long exchange, he answers Bram's question: "Old ones want to see three things. See tall ones' faces. See if tall ones come with night-quiet, like the four, or with talking." He glances at the light. "And see the light. Tisk says that one most."

From behind the hides the small cough comes again, wet and tired. The scarred male says something sharp toward it. Tisk lifts one claw and he stops. She clicks at Ixx, looking at Sefa.

"Small one is Pikk," Ixx says. "Smoke cough. When the fire is bad, every time." He listens again. "Tisk says: after. Maybe."

Then Tisk turns her good eye from the light on the shield to Sefa on the step and asks something that makes the scarred male shift his feet. Ixx hesitates before he passes it on, and when he does, he says it carefully.

"Tisk asks Sefa. Who gave the tall one this light?"
````

Together after: [Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore]

## Round 7

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 7 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram keeps his place and his shield still, but when Tisk looks to Sefa, he doesn't answer for her. He lets the quiet hold.

He turns his sword hand palm out, fingers open, and keeps it at his side where the scarred male can see it. Then he tips his head a fraction toward Sefa, voice low but not secretive.

"Your light. Your answer."

His eyes go back to the spear-kobold, steady, not challenging. His feet stay set between the elders and the stair. The heat from the red passage presses at the back of his neck, but he doesn't turn toward it. Whatever comes out of the hot rooms, he wants to hear it before he turns his back on this room.

Sefa Marrin: Sefa doesn't answer at once. She looks at the old one's dust-grey palm, then down at her own hands open on her knees, and for a moment she's quiet in the way of someone deciding not to lie to a sick woman.

"I'll give you the true answer, Old Tisk, because you asked it plainly." She keeps her voice in that low ward-register, and lets Ixx carry it. "Nobody gave it. I asked, and something answered. I was a sister in a sick-house in the Gate, the year the fever came off the docks. The ninth night the oil ran out and the ward went dark, and people were dying in the dark where I couldn't see to help them. I prayed to anyone who was listening. A voice came, like a lamp being lit. It has been in my hands since." She turns one hand over; nothing shows in it, but her eyes go to the small yellow pool on Bram's shield. "I don't know its name. I call it the Lantern. It left me a book, and it left me a debt I've never been told the size of." A breath. "I did not know, until I put my hand on your door this afternoon, that anyone else had ever carved its picture in stone."

She lets that sit, then meets the milky eye. "Ixx says the deep door has a light like mine. Small and yellow. I'd like you to tell me about it, if the old ones say it's a thing for tall ones to hear. And I'd like to know who lent the light the lintel speaks of — *by lent light* — and to whom."

Only then, quieter, with a glance at the hides where the cough came from: "Pikk's cough is in his chest. Smoke does that to the small and the old first. After, if you'll have me, I'll look. And I saw the iron cap on your peg — the one that ran like wax. I'd be glad if someone told me how a helmet comes to do that before I go down the warm passage and find out for myself."

Low, aside, for Bram and Pell only: "That helmet melted on a man's head. Whatever killed the four, it breathes. Mind the red."

Pell Dunmore: I crouch, press my empty palm to the dusty floor, and hold it up toward Tisk as she did. “Pell Dunmore.”

Then I leave her question to Sefa.

Quietly to Bram, without pointing: “Boot tracks into the burned room. Several, roughly ten days old. I can’t see any coming out. The clan’s tracks stop at the dust.” I keep my hands open. “Worth asking about before we go farther.”
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Ixx translates Sefa's answer a piece at a time. He stops after each part, clicks it across to Tisk and waits, and twice he goes back for a word he didn't have. *Sick-house* takes him a while. *Ninth night* he gives with his claws held up. At *debt*, Tisk's hand leaves the light for the first time and goes to the shells at her throat. Her claws close over the three turns of them and stay closed.

While Ixx finishes, Bram's open hand hangs at his side. The scarred male's eyes go to it, then back to the hilt, then to the hand again, as if he's checking both are still where he left them. The spear stays upright.

Pell crouches, presses his palm to the floor and holds it up grey. Tisk turns her good eye on him. She clicks something short, and one of the kobolds holding the hides makes a sound like a laugh caught halfway.

"Tisk says Pell learns quick," Ixx says. "Pell Dunmore." The scarred male snorts through his nose and says nothing.

Then Tisk answers, and Ixx gives it to them in pieces as she does.

"The cap." He points with his snout at the hides, where the peg hangs. "Four tall ones went to the hot rooms, night-quiet. The hot rooms did that. Broom found them after. Carried up. Burned proper." A rasp from Tisk, and he adds: "Cap stays on the peg so the small ones see it and do not go play in the hot rooms."

She says nothing about who or what was in the hot rooms when it happened, and neither does Ixx.

When Sefa's other questions reach her, about the door's light and who lent it, Tisk doesn't answer straight away. She turns her head to the wide passage, toward the red glow and the slow warm breath rising out of it, and keeps her head turned for a long moment. When she speaks again her clicks have gone so soft that Ixx bends right down to hear.

"Tisk asks Sefa one thing." He straightens up and says it carefully. "Your Lantern. Did it send you to this door?"

Something moves in the red passage before anyone can answer.

It's not a step. The breath that has risen past them all afternoon stops, the way a man stops breathing when he's listening. For a heartbeat the air in the dome goes still and close. Then it comes out again in one long gust, much hotter, like a forge door opened and shut. It pushes Sefa's hair back from her face and stirs the dust fan in front of the burned arch. Under it, so low it's felt in the stone more than heard, comes a sound like a great bellows emptying.

Every kobold in the room goes still. Behind the hides, Pikk stops coughing mid-breath.

"He waits," Ixx says quietly. He doesn't look at the passage.

Tisk lets go of her shells. She clicks fast at Ixx, and the scarred male answers her, sharp and long, with his spear tilting a finger's width toward the stair. She answers him with one dry word. He shuts his mouth, but the spear doesn't come back upright.

"Tisk says: not here. Here the warm passage hears." Ixx swallows. "Inside, by the fire. Sefa looks at Pikk. Tisk talks of the door." He glances at the scarred male. "Hrask says iron stays outside the hides. No iron by the small ones. Sefa has no iron." He looks at the dagger on the step, then at Bram's hip and Pell's. "Bram and Pell come with no iron, or Bram and Pell wait here, by the warm passage."

Tisk is already turning toward the hides on her blackened stick. Then she stops, half turned, and looks back over her shoulder at Sefa. She is still waiting for an answer to her question.

 *(Out of game: none of these needed a die, and all three happened as declared. Bram holds his place, keeps his shield still, shows his open hand and says "Your light. Your answer." to Sefa, and Hrask's eyes keep going between his hand and his hilt. Sefa speaks the truth, so nothing gets rolled, and Ixx carries all of her answer, pausing as he goes; her questions about the deep door's light and the lent light reach Tisk, and Tisk puts off answering them until they're inside. Pell's dust-hand is shown in the narration and Tisk answers it with "Pell learns quick.")*
````
