## Round 8

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 8 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram holds still while the last of that hot gust drags across the stone. He doesn't look away from the red passage until the air settles, then turns his head just enough to watch the elders again.

"Answer her, Sefa. Same truth as before." He says it low, at her shoulder, not pulling her eyes off Tisk.

Then he speaks to the old one through Ixx, and to Hrask plainly.

"I won't carry iron into your home. Longsword and javelins can wait at the curtain, points down. But I'm wearing iron from neck to knee—it's not something a man shrugs off in a doorway, and I'm not going to stand out here by the warm passage while Sefa sits by your fire. If the shirt bars me, I'll keep to the inside wall, away from your little ones. My shield stays on my arm. No blade drawn, not in your home. You have my word."

He unclasps his sword belt deliberately and lowers the longsword to the floor beside the hide curtain, scabbard and all, then takes the four javelins from the quiver at his back and leans them beside it, points angled down. He keeps his eyes on Hrask while he does it, smooth and careful, and leaves his shield exactly where it is on his left arm. The warmth from the red passage is still breathing at the nape of his neck.

"Pell, leave yours with mine unless their old ones say otherwise. We'll talk with our hands open and our backs near the door."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa gets up from the step slowly, so nothing about it looks like a start, and leaves the dagger lying where it is, hilt away from the hides. The hot gust lifts her hair and she lets it fall again without turning to look at the passage. Only her thumb moves on the beads.

"No, Old Tisk." She says it to the milky eye, and then waits for Ixx. "I'd like to say yes. It would make a better story, and it might make you trust me faster. But the Lantern doesn't speak to me in words. It hasn't told me to come here, or to leave, or anything at all. We came because coin was offered for a ruin and the road was quiet." She looks down at the yellow pool on Bram's shield, then back. "What I can tell you is that it's been very near me since I touched your lintel. The light dipped on the stair a moment, like a lamp in a draught. I've not felt it do that in six years. If it sent me, it did it without asking — which, from what I know of it, is how it does most things."

She turns to the others, keeping her voice ordinary. "I'm going in. I've no iron to leave, and a child in there stopped coughing because he's afraid to breathe." A beat. "I'd rather not have us all unarmed in one room *and* nobody watching the passage. Bram — you're the one it would take longest to get a sword back onto; stay by the stair with your steel and the light, and keep an eye on that red for me. Pell, your choice. If you come, your blades lie on the step beside mine where Bram can see them, and you hear what Tisk says about the door with your own ears, which I'd value. If you stay, I'll tell you every word." She picks up her pack. "Either way — if I call, don't come through the hides swinging. Call back. I'll have a child in my hands."

Then to Ixx: "Tell Hrask I heard him. No iron by the small ones. I'll want water, clean if you have it, and whatever Pikk's been breathing at — I want to see the fire."

She touches two fingers to the rim of Bram's shield as she passes, and the Light goes out there and wakes again, smaller and softer, cupped in her own palm. *(Recasting Light into my hand, dim — cantrip.)*

Pell Dunmore: I stand slowly. “I’ll go in with Sefa, if she’s willing. Bram, would you mind our gear? I’d rather she had someone beside her.”

To Ixx and Hrask: “No weapons by the small ones. Understood. My armour has iron studs, and my tools are metal too—do those stay out?”

I begin unbuckling my sword belt and gathering my daggers, bow and arrows beside the stair, without reaching into the hides. I wait for their answer before removing anything else.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
The hot gust thins out across the floor and is gone. The slow breath from the passage settles back to its old pace, and Bram doesn't take his eyes off the red until it has.

He says his piece, and Ixx turns it into clicks a little at a time. At *neck to knee* he touches his own throat and then his knee to show what Bram means. Then Bram unbuckles his sword belt and every kobold at the curtain watches him do it. He lowers the longsword to the floor beside the hides, scabbard and all. He takes the four javelins from the quiver on his back one at a time and leans them against the wall with the points down. He watches Hrask the whole time. Hrask's eyes follow the hilt down to the floor and stay on it for a moment after Bram straightens up. Then, for the first time since he came out, they leave it and move to the shield on Bram's arm, then to the mail over his chest. He says nothing. Tisk doesn't answer Bram either. Her face is still turned to Sefa, and she is waiting.

Sefa stands up from the step and answers her, and Ixx passes it on more slowly than before. Tisk hears it through to the end. When Ixx gets to *without asking*, she closes her good eye, so both are shut, and for a moment she is just a very old kobold leaning on a burnt stick. Then she opens it again and clicks something short.

"Tisk hears," Ixx says. "Inside, she tells Sefa why she asks."

Sefa says what she wants to Bram and Pell and picks up her pack. On her way past she touches two fingers to the rim of Bram's shield. The light there goes out, and the dome sinks back to the dull red at the end of the passage and a thin line of firelight at the gap in the hides. Then the light wakes again in her cupped hand, softened by her fingers. It's enough to show the floor at her feet and light her chin from below. Ixx gives Hrask her words about the small ones, then calls her request through the hides. Someone inside answers in a rasp.

Pell gets up and unbuckles his belt. He lays the shortsword, both daggers, the unstrung bow and the quiver at the foot of the stair beside Sefa's knife, then asks his question and keeps his hands still.

Ixx turns from the tall ones to the elders and back, carrying three things at once. Tisk and Hrask go at it quickly. Tisk says one thing, Hrask says a lot, and Tisk says the last word.

"Tisk says shirts come," Ixx says. "Bram's iron shirt. Pell's shirt with little studs. A shirt does not cut. The round wood on Bram's arm comes too." He looks sideways at Hrask. "Hrask says then Hrask stands by Bram. By the door, close, all the time Bram is inside." Hrask has already moved. He stands inside the hides next to the doorpost with his spear upright, and the space he has left beside him is exactly wide enough for a man in mail.

Ixx points a claw at the roll of oiled cloth on Pell's belt. "Pell's little iron stays out. Hrask says small ones take small shiny things, and then Hrask has to go and take them back." One of the kobolds holding the hides open looks down at its feet.

Tisk ducks through the gap, and Sefa follows her in.

The warren is an old cellar with a vault of dressed stone. At the crown it's a little more than a man's height, and lower at the walls. It's hot in the close way a full room is hot. A fire of dung cakes burns in a ring of stones in the middle. Its smoke goes up in a straight grey thread to a black hole in the vault and out. The air under the ceiling is hazy but not thick. Along the walls are the kobolds of the Broom. Sefa counts twenty and stops counting. A lot of them are small, and they shrink back from the light in her hand and then lean toward it again. The melted cap hangs on its peg at her shoulder as she passes.

Pikk is easy to find. A she-kobold sits on a heap of hides by the fire, holding something about the size of a cat, wrapped up to the snout. He breathes through an open mouth, fast, with a thin whistle at the end of each breath. His chest jerks with the effort of holding the cough in, and his eyes are fixed on Sefa. A kobold puts a clay pot of water by Sefa's feet and backs off. The water is clear and as warm as everything else down here.

Ixx stays in the doorway, half in and half out, so that both halves of the talk can reach him. He passes on what Sefa asked to see and brings back Tisk's answer.

"Not this fire. This fire is for meat." He tips his snout back over his shoulder, toward the dome and the red beyond it. "The fire Pikk breathes is down the warm passage. When the hot rooms breathe bad, the bad comes up, comes in here. The smoke-hole does not take it."

Now that she's close to the child, Sefa catches a smell under the goat and dung smoke. It's thin and sharp, like flint just after it's been struck, and it was on the stair too, before she had a name for it. It's on Pikk's breath.

Outside, the dome is dark apart from the red at the end of the passage and the firelight through the gap. Hrask stands at the doorpost with the empty place beside him, and he doesn't move.

*(Out of game: Light has to sit on an object, not on skin, so it's on whatever small thing Sefa holds in that hand. Tell me what it is.)*

 *(Out of game: none of the four needed a die, and the narration already plays each one as you declared it. Bram's words reach the elders through Ixx, his longsword and javelins lie by the curtain, Sefa tells the truth (and the truth is never rolled), and her Light leaves Bram's shield and wakes in her cupped hand.)*
````

## Round 9

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 9 — one paragraph per character in this scene. Sefa Marrin is elsewhere this round, in a scene of their own that comes to you as its own message: do not narrate that character here.)

Bram Halloway: Bram doesn't cross the threshold. He steps up to the open doorway and takes the space at Hrask's side with his shield angled outward toward the dim red at the end of the passage, not into the den where the small ones are. From the doorway he can see inside—Sefa, Pell, the fire, the room—and he can still turn his head and cover the dome and the stair behind him.

He leaves his sword and javelins on the floor just to his left, within a stride, and folds his arms over his chest, careful, so the mail doesn't clatter.

"Then I'll keep the door," he says to Ixx and Hrask. "Your roof, your rules. I can see the passage from here, and I can hear her." He glances once at Hrask, not hostile. "You don't have to stand close. I don't move from this spot unless I'm called or there's iron in that passage."

He lets his eyes go past the curtain: to Sefa's light as she kneels near the child, to Pell's profile, to Tisk's face. And every few breaths his head turns back toward the red glow, marking the rhythm of that slow exhalation from the hot rooms.

Pell Dunmore: I set my thieves’ tools with the weapons. “Fair enough. I’d want the shiny things back too.”

Then I follow Sefa through the hides, keeping my hands visible and watching my head beneath the low roof. I settle near her without getting between her and Pikk, leaving the way to the doorway clear.

I let Tisk speak, listening closely for what she knows about the door and Sefa’s light.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Hrask doesn't take a step back when Bram comes up beside him. Ixx, half in and half out of the hides, passes on *you don't have to stand close*. Hrask hears it out with his eyes on the mail. Then he plants the butt of his spear on the stone a hand's width from Bram's boot and leaves it there. He says something short. Ixx doesn't translate it, and from the look of him it didn't need translating.

What Hrask does next he does without a word. He turns his body a quarter, so he's no longer facing Bram but standing next to him, and he looks out the same way Bram does: across the dark dome toward the dull red at the end of the passage. The two of them stand at the doorpost watching the same thing.

From there Bram has both rooms. On his right are the smoky warren, the dung fire, Sefa's small light by the hearth and the rows of eyes along the walls. On his left are the dome, the stair, his sword and javelins a stride away on the floor, and the passage. He picks up the passage's breathing the way he'd pick up a team's breathing on a long grade. It's slow and even, a long draw and a longer letting-go, and he counts it against his own heartbeat. It holds steady for a while. Then one breath out comes a beat early, as if whatever's down there shifted its weight. After that it settles back into its old pace. Nobody else seems to have noticed.

Pell lays the roll of oiled cloth beside the weapons. As he says his piece about shiny things, the small kobold holding the hides open looks at the tools on the floor, then quickly up at the ceiling, and tucks both hands behind its back. Hrask doesn't turn his head, but his tail flicks once at the little one, and it goes red to the ear-frills.

Pell ducks under the hides. The vault is low enough near the door that he has to keep his head bent for the first few steps. He finds a place by the wall, a little behind and to one side of the fire, close enough to hear Tisk and well clear of the child and the woman holding him. The doorway stays open to his left, with Bram's mail filling half of it.

Reading a room is his trade, so he does it while he waits.

The kobolds along the walls watch him and then, slowly, go back to what they were doing. One is twisting gut into cord. Two are scraping a goat hide pegged out flat on the floor. One is feeding dung cakes to the fire from a basket.

At the back of the warren, where the vault drops lowest, a gap in the stone leads to a second, smaller cellar. Something moves in there, and the smell gives it away before the shapes do: two goats, tethered to a ring in the wall, brown and white, chewing. They're a farmer's goats, not wild ones. One has a neat V-notch cut in its left ear, and that's an owner's mark anywhere on the Sword Coast. Two long poles lean against the wall next to the gap, with crosspieces lashed between them. Their undersides are worn smooth and pale where they've been dragged over stone, many times.

By the door, under the peg with the melted cap, there's a low shelf cut into the wall. On it sits a shallow basket woven from the same peeled twigs as Ixx's broom, and in the basket is what the fire didn't take. There are four belt buckles. Two of them are slumped and run at one edge, like the cap. There's the iron toe-cap of a boot, scorched blue-black. And there are bones: a jaw, some small bones of a hand, part of a long bone from a leg, all of them blackened right through, the way bone goes when it's been in a very hot fire. Someone has laid them out in rows with care, not just dumped them.

Tisk has lowered herself onto a folded hide by the fire. She props the blackened stick across her knees and turns her good eye toward the hearth, where the child is. For a long moment she just watches. Then she clicks something low to Ixx without looking away.

"Tisk says: first Pikk," Ixx says from the doorway, loud enough for the whole room. "Then the door. She waits."

She sits and waits. The child whistles at the end of each breath. Out in the dome the passage breathes in, long and slow.
````

````text
The thief sets his roll of oiled cloth down beside your weapons. "Fair enough. I'd want the shiny things back too." He follows Sefa through the hides with his hands in plain view and his head bowed under the low roof.

Then he plants the butt of his spear on the stone a hand's width from your boot and leaves it there. He says something short. Ixx doesn't translate it, and from the look of him it didn't need translating.

What Hrask does next he does without a word. He turns his body a quarter, so he's no longer facing you but standing next to you, and he looks out the same way you do: across the dark dome toward the dull red at the end of the passage. The two of you stand at the doorpost watching the same thing.

From there you have both rooms. On your right are the smoky warren, the dung fire, Sefa's small light by the hearth and the rows of eyes along the walls. On your left are the dome, the stair, your sword and javelins a stride away on the floor, and the passage. You pick up the passage's breathing the way you'd pick up a team's breathing on a long grade. It's slow and even, a long draw and a longer letting-go, and you count it against your own heartbeat. It holds steady for a while. Then one breath out comes a beat early, as if whatever's down there shifted its weight. After that it settles back into its old pace. Nobody else seems to have noticed.

When the thief laid his tools down and spoke of shiny things, the small kobold holding the hides open looked at the tools on the floor, then quickly up at the ceiling, and tucked both hands behind its back. Hrask didn't turn his head, but his tail flicked once at the little one, and it went red to the ear-frills.

Inside, the thief keeps his head bent under the low vault for the first few steps. He finds a place by the wall, a little behind and to one side of the fire, well clear of the child and the woman holding him. The kobolds along the walls watch him and then, slowly, go back to what they were doing. One is twisting gut into cord. Two are scraping a goat hide pegged out flat on the floor. One is feeding dung cakes to the fire from a basket.

"Tisk says: first Pikk," Ixx says from the doorway, loud enough for the whole room. "Then the door. She waits."

She sits and waits. The child whistles at the end of each breath. Out in the dome the passage breathes in, long and slow.
````

**Pell Dunmore's screen (the relay):**

````text
Bram doesn't cross the threshold. He steps up to the open doorway and takes the space at Hrask's side, his shield angled outward toward the dim red at the end of the passage. He leaves his sword and javelins on the floor just to his left, within a stride, and folds his arms over his chest carefully, so the mail doesn't clatter.

"Then I'll keep the door," he says to Ixx and Hrask. "Your roof, your rules. I can see the passage from here, and I can hear her." He glances once at Hrask. "You don't have to stand close. I don't move from this spot unless I'm called or there's iron in that passage."

Hrask doesn't step back when Bram comes up beside him. Ixx, half in and half out of the hides, passes on *you don't have to stand close*. Hrask hears it out with his eyes on the mail. Then he plants the butt of his spear on the stone a hand's width from Bram's boot and leaves it there. He says something short. Ixx doesn't translate it, and from the look of him it didn't need translating. Then Hrask turns a quarter, so he's standing next to Bram instead of facing him, and looks out the same way Bram does, across the dark dome toward the dull red at the end of the passage.

You lay the roll of oiled cloth beside the weapons. As you say your piece about shiny things, the small kobold holding the hides open looks at the tools on the floor, then quickly up at the ceiling, and tucks both hands behind its back. Hrask doesn't turn his head, but his tail flicks once at the little one, and it goes red to the ear-frills.

You duck under the hides. The vault is low enough near the door that you have to keep your head bent for the first few steps. You find a place by the wall, a little behind and to one side of the fire, close enough to hear Tisk and well clear of the child and the woman holding him. The doorway stays open to your left, with Bram's mail filling half of it.

Reading a room is your trade, so you do it while you wait.

The kobolds along the walls watch you and then, slowly, go back to what they were doing. One is twisting gut into cord. Two are scraping a goat hide pegged out flat on the floor. One is feeding dung cakes to the fire from a basket.

At the back of the warren, where the vault drops lowest, a gap in the stone leads to a second, smaller cellar. Something moves in there, and the smell gives it away before the shapes do: two goats, tethered to a ring in the wall, brown and white, chewing. They're a farmer's goats, not wild ones. One has a neat V-notch cut in its left ear, and that's an owner's mark anywhere on the Sword Coast. Two long poles lean against the wall next to the gap, with crosspieces lashed between them. Their undersides are worn smooth and pale where they've been dragged over stone, many times.

By the door, under the peg with the melted cap, there's a low shelf cut into the wall. On it sits a shallow basket woven from the same peeled twigs as Ixx's broom, and in the basket is what the fire didn't take. There are four belt buckles. Two of them are slumped and run at one edge, like the cap. There's the iron toe-cap of a boot, scorched blue-black. And there are bones: a jaw, some small bones of a hand, part of a long bone from a leg, all of them blackened right through, the way bone goes when it's been in a very hot fire. Someone has laid them out in rows with care, not just dumped them.

Tisk has lowered herself onto a folded hide by the fire. She props the blackened stick across her knees and turns her good eye toward the hearth, where the child is. For a long moment she just watches. Then she clicks something low to Ixx without looking away.

"Tisk says: first Pikk," Ixx says from the doorway, loud enough for the whole room. "Then the door. She waits."

She sits and waits. The child whistles at the end of each breath. Out in the dome the passage breathes in, long and slow.

*(Out of game: no roll was needed. You set your tools down with the weapons, go in after Sefa with your hands in plain view and your head bowed under the vault, and sit by the wall where you don't block Pikk or the doorway.)*
````

### Scene: Sefa Marrin (order: Sefa)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 9 — one paragraph per character in this scene. Bram Halloway and Pell Dunmore are elsewhere this round, in a scene of their own that comes to you as its own message: do not narrate them here.)

Sefa Marrin: *(The Light is on the prayer beads — I slipped them off my neck and have them wound round my fingers in that hand, so it's the beads that glow, cupped.)*

Sefa kneels by the heap of hides and sets the pot of water beside her knee, not between her and the mother. She keeps the glowing beads low, so the child sees her face lit from below and not a flame coming at him.

"Hello, Pikk. I'm Sefa. I'm going to look at you, and I'm going to be slow about it." She says it in Common, for the tone, and lets Ixx give the mother the sense of it. Then, to the mother, with her hands still: "May I?" — and she waits for the nod before she touches.

When she has it, she works the way she was taught: loosens the wrapping from his chest so the ribs can move, props him more upright against his mother's arm instead of flat, and lays the back of two fingers against his throat and then his chest — feeling for heat and for how the breath moves under the whistle. She bends close and smells his breath on purpose, not flinching from it, and then smells the air near the floor, and then near the smoke-hole. *(Medicine check if you want one — Wisdom, not proficient. I'm also trying to put a name to that flint-struck smell with what I know of the arcane and the Faith: brimstone, dragonbreath, something else? Arcana or Religion, whichever you think fits.)*

While her hands are busy she talks to Tisk through Ixx, quiet, not stopping her work: "Say it now, Old Tisk, while the passage can't hear. Why did you ask who gave me the light — and what's the light on the deep door?"

To the mother and Ixx, as she goes: "Hot water, close under his snout, with a hide over both their heads to hold the steam — like that. It loosens the chest. When the hot rooms breathe bad, carry the small ones *up* the stair, past the string, as far as the bend. The bad air is heavy; it lies low and comes in at the floor. Higher is cleaner." A pause, honest. "I can take the sickness out of his chest with a word. But the word costs me something I may need before this day is done, and I won't spend it until I've heard what's at the bottom of your passage. So tell me, and I'll decide with the whole picture in front of me — not instead of helping him. As well as."
````

**Card** · Sefa Marrin · Arcana: naming the struck-flint smell on Pikk's breath and at the floor · roll · presses: [Roll the die]

**Card** · Sefa Marrin · Arcana: naming the struck-flint smell on Pikk's breath and at the floor · decide · presses: [Onward]

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Sefa takes the beads off her neck and winds them round her fingers. The Light goes with them, a soft yellow glow caught between her knuckles. She kneels on the hides with the pot of water by her knee, and the glow lights her face from below and not much else.

Ixx says her words to the mother from the doorway, more softly than Sefa said them. The mother is young, with dull scales and dust in the creases of her snout. She looks at the beads, then at Sefa's face, and holds the look for some time. Then she ducks her head once and loosens her hold on the bundle by a finger's width.

Sefa's hands know this work. She did it a hundred nights on the ward, and a small chest is a small chest, whatever scales are on it. She unwinds the wrapping from his ribs, sits him up against his mother's arm and lays the backs of two fingers on his throat, then on his chest. He's warm, but no warmer than anything else down here, so there's no fever. The whistle is high in his chest, not deep, and his ribs pull in hard under her fingers on every breath, the way a child's do when the air itself has scraped the inside raw. It isn't a sickness that has taken hold of him. Something he breathed has hurt him, it keeps hurting, and he's too small to cough it out. Pikk watches her the whole time with his mouth open. When her fingers move he doesn't flinch, and once his own small claw closes on a bead and holds it.

She bends to his snout and breathes in on purpose. Struck flint again, sharp and clean. She checks the floor next, down by the hem of the hides where the doorway lets in the dome's air, and the smell is there, faint. Up by the smoke-hole there's only dung smoke and goat.

She tries to place it. The smell isn't new to her, only its name has slipped her.

She has it. It isn't brimstone, and by every book she's read it isn't a beast either. Writers who have stood near a dragon's breath agree on how it smells: smoke and musk, scorched meat, the rot of something that lives on flesh. This smell has none of that in it. It's clean and mineral, with nothing alive in it, like stone heated hotter than stone should ever be. The books put that smell around fire that was *called* rather than lit: a bound elemental, a brazier opened onto the Plane of Fire, a working that feeds on heat. The fumes it gives off are heavy, and they creep along floors and settle in low rooms. That's why the smoke-hole can't clear them and the smell sits at the hem of the hides. Whatever is poisoning Pikk is down the red passage. As far as Sefa can tell from his breath, though, it isn't the breath of anything with scales.

She keeps her hands moving and asks her question while she works. Ixx carries it to the fire. Tisk listens with her stick across her knees and her good eye on the child, and takes her time answering.

"Tisk asks because of the deep door," Ixx says. "On the door, the lantern. Cut in stone, like over the up-door. Inside it, a flame. Not oil. Not fire. Yellow." He looks at the beads in Sefa's fingers. "Like that."

Tisk clicks again and holds up one claw, curled round as if there were an egg in it.

"When Tisk was small, the flame was so big." She draws the claw tight until it's nearly shut, then points at the bead Pikk has hold of. "Now so big. Every year smaller. Every year the stair warmer." Ixx swallows. "Tisk does not know the words over the up-door. Sefa says them. *Lent.* Sefa says *debt.* Sefa says *sent without asking.*" Tisk is quiet for a moment. "A thing that is lent, someone comes for it. So Tisk asks."

Sefa's other words go across: hot water, a hide over the head, carrying the small ones up the stair as far as the bend. The mother looks at Tisk before she does anything. Tisk clicks once. A kobold by the wall takes the clay pot and sets it at the edge of the dung fire to heat. Another brings a soft goat hide and drapes it over the mother's shoulders, ready.

"Up to the bend is dark," Ixx says, mostly to himself. "Is not sun. Is good." He watches the mother draw the hide closer. "Tisk hears the word that costs. Tisk says Sefa keeps it. Pikk has coughed many times. Pikk is still here."

Then Tisk lifts her claws to the shells at her throat and closes them over all three turns. Ixx bends to her. When he straightens up he waits a moment before speaking.

"Tisk asks one more. When Sefa stands at the deep door, with this light..." He keeps his eyes off the hides. "...whose hand is it? Sefa's hand, or the Lantern's?"

Before anyone can answer, heat pushes in under the hem of the hides. It isn't a gust this time, just a long warm swell that rolls in across the floor and carries the struck-flint smell with it, strong enough that Sefa tastes it. Along the walls the kobolds go still, every one of them, hands stopped on cord and hide and dung basket. Every snout in the room turns to the edge of the curtain and stays there.

Against his mother's arm, Pikk takes one whistling breath and starts to cough.

*(Out of game: reading a sick child's chest is your hospice trade, so that part needed no card.)*

 *(Out of game: I didn't roll Medicine because the player left it optional and reading a sick child's chest is Sefa's hospice trade, so it worked without a card. The story already gives her reading of Pikk: no fever, a whistle high in the chest, an airway scraped raw by something he breathed rather than a sickness.)*
````

## Round 10

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 10 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram stays where he is, arms folded, shield angled toward the darkness. When the passage's breath comes early, his eyes narrow, and he lets it settle before he speaks.

"One of those breaths came short a moment back," he says, low enough that it doesn't carry into the den. "Like it shifted." He doesn't look at Ixx, but it's meant for him. "Is it sleeping down there, or is it listening to us through the stone?"

He shifts his weight from heel to heel, not far enough to leave the doorway, and glances once through the curtain: Sefa's light low by the child, Pell along the wall, Tisk waiting by the fire. They're not in a room he can cover with his shield, but they're within his eyeline. That will do for now.

Sefa Marrin: The heat rolls in across the floor and Sefa doesn't turn to look at it either. She pulls the pot off the fire's edge with the hem of her sleeve — it's only warm, not hot enough yet to help — and sets it under Pikk's snout anyway, then draws the goat hide up over the mother's head and the child's together, leaving a gap for her own hand.

"Breathe over the water, little one. Slow as you can." Her other hand goes flat on the small heaving chest under the hide, and the glow of the beads brightens for a moment between her knuckles — not a flare, a lamp turned up a notch. *(Spending two dice of Healing Light on Pikk — bonus action, 2d6, so 4/6 left. No slot.)*

"That's not the word that costs," she tells the mother, through Ixx, when the glow settles. "That's just the light. It should take the rawness out of his chest for now. It won't stop the bad air coming in — nothing I carry does that. Only the stair does."

Then she sits back on her heels and gives Tisk her answer, because the old one has waited through two questions already.

"My hand, Old Tisk. Everything I've ever done with this light, I chose to do — the Lantern has never moved my fingers for me. If it wants a thing opened, or kept shut, it will have to tell me so plainly, and I'll still decide." A breath. "But I'll be honest about the other half. I don't know what the debt is. If the thing you said is true — that what is lent, someone comes for — then I may be the one it's come to collect from, and I'd rather find that out standing at your door with my eyes open than have it find me asleep in the Gate."

She pitches the next part so Pell hears it too, and so Ixx can carry it to Bram at the doorpost. "That smell isn't a beast. Every book I've read that stood near dragonfire says smoke, musk, scorched meat. This is clean stone heated past what stone can bear — *called* fire. A bound elemental, a working on the Plane of Fire, something that eats heat. Its fumes are heavy; that's why they come in at the floor and the smoke-hole can't take them." She looks at Tisk. "Your deep door has a lantern that's been going out for a lifetime, and the stair gets warmer every year it fades. I think the lantern on that door is *holding* something, Tisk, and it's running out. And your Warm One has red scales and can't climb his own stair, and wants tall hands to open it."

Quietly: "So before I go down there I want to know two things. Does the Warm One know the flame is dying? And does he want the door opened to let something *out* — or to get *in* before the light goes?"

She gets up, slow, and lifts the beads so the mother can see she's taking the glow with her. "Keep the hide on them till the water cools. Then up to the bend, all the small ones, whenever the floor breathes like that. Pell — I think we go and hear him. But if he lies to me about that smell, I'll know it, and we walk back up."

Pell Dunmore: I ease the healer’s satchel off my shoulder and set it beside me.

“Sefa, three healing draughts here if you need one,” I say quietly. “Tell me what else would help.”

I stay out of her way and keep still, giving Pikk one less stranger looming over him. While Tisk waits, I wait with her.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Ixx's head turns at Bram's low voice. He doesn't look at the passage, the same as before.

"He does not sleep much," Ixx says, just as low. He touches the doorpost with two claws, then the floor. "Stone carries. The old ones say the stone is his ear. When we talk loud by the warm passage, he breathes different." He lets out a thin breath through his nose. "Ixx thinks he listens. Ixx does not know. Ixx only knows he breathes different."

Next to Bram, Hrask doesn't need any of that translated. His spear stays upright, but his tail has curled round the doorpost.

Inside, Sefa takes the pot off the edge of the fire with her sleeve and slides it under Pikk's snout. The mother lets her draw the hide up over both their heads, and stays still under it like a tent. Sefa's free hand goes in under the hide's edge and rests flat on the small heaving chest. Between her knuckles the beads brighten, slowly and steadily, like a lamp wick turned up a notch.

It isn't much light, but Pikk isn't much of a body, and it's enough. Under her palm the hard pull of his ribs eases with the next breath, then the one after. The whistle doesn't go away, but it drops from a thin shriek to a soft wheeze, the sound of a child with a cold, not a child fighting for air. The cough that started a moment ago breaks off. Pikk breathes over the warm water with his mouth open, and his claw is still closed on the bead.

From under the hide the mother makes a small sound. It isn't a word.

Ixx gives the mother what Sefa said, about the light and the stair. Then Sefa sits back on her heels and gives Tisk her answer, and Ixx carries that across too, a piece at a time.

Tisk listens to *my hand* with her good eye on Sefa's face. At *I may be the one it's come to collect from*, the old kobold's head tilts a little to one side, the way a bird's does. She says nothing until the whole answer is across, the smell and the books and the fading lantern included. Then she lets out a long dry click.

"Tisk hears. Sefa's hand." Ixx straightens. "Sefa says the true thing and the bad thing together. Tisk likes this more than only the true thing."

She answers the questions one at a time, the way Ixx does.

"Does he know the flame dies." Tisk lays a claw against her milky eye, then her good one. "He knows. He lies by the door. When the flame was big he did not look at it. Now he looks every sleep. Long."

The second question takes her longer. She looks into the dung fire, and when she clicks again it's so soft that Ixx bends nearly to her snout.

"Under the door is the fire-not-fire. It wants nothing. Fire only eats." Ixx swallows. "The Warm One is not under the door. The Warm One wants up." Tisk raises one claw and draws a line in the air, from the floor up toward the vault. "When Tisk already had eggs, the Warm One came down the stair. Small. Like a goat. Hungry. He followed the warm." She lowers the claw. "Down is easy."

She stops there, and from the way her jaw sets she means to.

Pell has his satchel off his shoulder and lying beside him on the hides, with the three red flasks showing at the mouth of it. Sefa doesn't need them, so nobody takes one. Pikk's mother has her head out from under the hide now, though, and her eyes go to the flasks and rest there for a moment the way the little door-holder's went to the tools. Then she looks at Pell. He's sitting still against the wall, out of the way, and her eyes stay on him a little longer than they did before.

Out in the dome the passage breathes in.

Bram hears it first: claws on stone, fast, coming up the red passage. Skra comes out of the red glow at a run, crosses the dome with her sling wound tight on her wrist and doesn't slow for the iron on the floor or the man at the doorpost. Hrask snaps one rasp at her as she goes past and she rasps back without stopping. Then she's through the gap in the hides, and Sefa's light falls on her heaving sides. She stops in the middle of the warren and speaks to the room, high and quick. Every kobold along the walls goes still again.

Ixx doesn't want to translate it. He looks at Tisk, and Tisk lifts her chin a finger's width.

"Skra brings his word," Ixx says. "The Warm One says…" His tail is around his ankle again. "'The tall ones talk long with the small ones. I am not small. The tall ones come now, or the tall ones go up, and the Broom goes with them.'" He stops. Skra rasps one more thing, and he adds, very quietly: "Skra says his breath was very hot when he said it."

Tisk doesn't move. Skra stands panting in the light and stares at the beads in Sefa's fingers, then at Sefa, waiting to see which of the tall ones answers.

 *(Out of game: none of the three needs another roll, and each one is already told in the turn. Sefa's Healing Light was rolled and it eased Pikk's chest. Her answer to Tisk is true speech, and true speech is never rolled. Tisk heard it and answered it. Bram's question to Ixx is speech too, and Ixx answered it at the doorpost.)*
````

The thin shriek of the child's breathing drops to a soft wheeze, the sound of a child with a cold. The cough that had started breaks off.

````text
Then Sefa sits back on her heels and talks with Tisk, and Ixx carries her words across a piece at a time. Part of it she pitches to carry: "That smell isn't a beast. Every book I've read that stood near dragonfire says smoke, musk, scorched meat. This is clean stone heated past what stone can bear — *called* fire. A bound elemental, a working on the Plane of Fire, something that eats heat. Its fumes are heavy; that's why they come in at the floor and the smoke-hole can't take them." She goes on about the deep door's lantern holding something that is running out, and about the Warm One with red scales who can't climb his own stair and wants tall hands to open it.

Tisk answers in clicks, and Ixx gives them in his own words: "Tisk hears. Sefa's hand. Sefa says the true thing and the bad thing together. Tisk likes this more than only the true thing." Then: "Does he know the flame dies. He knows. He lies by the door. When the flame was big he did not look at it. Now he looks every sleep. Long." And, after a long pause: "Under the door is the fire-not-fire. It wants nothing. Fire only eats. The Warm One is not under the door. The Warm One wants up. When Tisk already had eggs, the Warm One came down the stair. Small. Like a goat. Hungry. He followed the warm. Down is easy." From the way the old kobold's jaw sets, she means to stop there.

Out in the dome the passage breathes in.

You hear it first: claws on stone, fast, coming up the red passage. Skra comes out of the red glow at a run and crosses the dome with her sling wound tight on her wrist. She doesn't slow for the iron on the floor or for you at the doorpost. Hrask snaps one rasp at her as she goes past and she rasps back without stopping. Then she's through the gap in the hides and standing in the middle of the warren, sides heaving in Sefa's light. She speaks to the room, high and quick, and every kobold along the walls goes still again.

Ixx doesn't want to translate it. He looks at Tisk, and Tisk lifts her chin a finger's width.

"Skra brings his word," Ixx says. "The Warm One says…" His tail is around his ankle again. "'The tall ones talk long with the small ones. I am not small. The tall ones come now, or the tall ones go up, and the Broom goes with them.'" Skra rasps one more thing, and he adds, very quietly: "Skra says his breath was very hot when he said it."

Tisk doesn't move. Skra stands panting and stares at the beads in Sefa's fingers, then at Sefa, waiting to see which of the tall ones answers.
````

**Sefa Marrin's screen (the relay):**

````text
Out at the doorpost Bram stands with his arms folded and his shield angled toward the darkness. He says something low to Ixx that doesn't carry in to you, and Ixx answers just as low. Bram shifts his weight from heel to heel without leaving the doorway, and glances once through the curtain at you.

Pell eases the healer's satchel off his shoulder and sets it beside him on the hides, the three red flasks showing at its mouth. "Sefa, three healing draughts here if you need one," he says quietly. "Tell me what else would help." Then he keeps still against the wall, out of your way.

The mother lets you draw the hide up over both their heads and stays still under it like a tent. Your free hand rests flat on the small heaving chest, and between your knuckles the beads brighten, slowly and steadily, like a lamp wick turned up a notch.

It isn't much light, but Pikk isn't much of a body, and it's enough. Under your palm the hard pull of his ribs eases with the next breath, then the one after. The whistle doesn't go away, but it drops from a thin shriek to a soft wheeze, the sound of a child with a cold, not a child fighting for air. The cough that started a moment ago breaks off. Pikk breathes over the warm water with his mouth open, and his claw is still closed on the bead.

From under the hide the mother makes a small sound. It isn't a word.

Ixx gives the mother what you said about the light and the stair. Then you give Tisk your answer, and Ixx carries it across a piece at a time.

Tisk listens to *my hand* with her good eye on your face. At *I may be the one it's come to collect from*, her head tilts a little to one side, the way a bird's does. She says nothing until the whole answer is across, the smell and the books and the fading lantern included. Then she lets out a long dry click.

"Tisk hears. Sefa's hand." Ixx straightens. "Sefa says the true thing and the bad thing together. Tisk likes this more than only the true thing."

She answers your questions one at a time, the way Ixx does.

"Does he know the flame dies." Tisk lays a claw against her milky eye, then her good one. "He knows. He lies by the door. When the flame was big he did not look at it. Now he looks every sleep. Long."

The second question takes her longer. She looks into the dung fire, and when she clicks again it's so soft that Ixx bends nearly to her snout.

"Under the door is the fire-not-fire. It wants nothing. Fire only eats." Ixx swallows. "The Warm One is not under the door. The Warm One wants up." Tisk raises one claw and draws a line in the air, from the floor up toward the vault. "When Tisk already had eggs, the Warm One came down the stair. Small. Like a goat. Hungry. He followed the warm." She lowers the claw. "Down is easy."

She stops there, and from the way her jaw sets she means to.

You don't need Pell's flasks, so nobody takes one. Pikk's mother has her head out from under the hide now, though. Her eyes go to the flasks and rest there a moment, the way the little door-holder's went to the tools. Then she looks at Pell, sitting still against the wall, and her eyes stay on him a little longer than they did before.

Out in the dome the passage breathes in.

Claws on stone, fast. Skra comes through the gap in the hides at a run, her sling wound tight on her wrist, and a rasp from Hrask outside follows her in. Your light falls on her heaving sides. She stops in the middle of the warren and speaks to the room, high and quick, and every kobold along the walls goes still again.

Ixx doesn't want to translate it. He looks at Tisk, and Tisk lifts her chin a finger's width.

"Skra brings his word," Ixx says. "The Warm One says…" His tail is around his ankle again. "'The tall ones talk long with the small ones. I am not small. The tall ones come now, or the tall ones go up, and the Broom goes with them.'" He stops. Skra rasps one more thing, and he adds, very quietly: "Skra says his breath was very hot when he said it."

Tisk doesn't move. Skra stands panting in the light and stares at the beads in your fingers, then at you, waiting to see which of the tall ones answers.
````

**Pell Dunmore's screen (the relay):**

````text
Out at the doorpost Bram stands with his arms folded and his shield angled toward the darkness. He says something low to Ixx that doesn't carry in to you, and Ixx answers just as low. Bram shifts his weight from heel to heel without leaving the doorway, and glances once through the curtain.

Sefa pulls the pot off the fire's edge with the hem of her sleeve and sets it under Pikk's snout, then draws the goat hide up over the mother's head and the child's together. The mother stays still under it like a tent. "Breathe over the water, little one. Slow as you can." Sefa's free hand goes in under the hide's edge, flat on the small heaving chest, and between her knuckles the beads brighten, slowly and steadily, like a lamp wick turned up a notch.

The child's breathing changes. The whistle drops from a thin shriek to a soft wheeze, the sound of a child with a cold, not a child fighting for air. The cough that started a moment ago breaks off. From under the hide the mother makes a small sound. It isn't a word.

"That's not the word that costs," Sefa tells the mother, and Ixx carries it across. "That's just the light. It should take the rawness out of his chest for now. It won't stop the bad air coming in — nothing I carry does that. Only the stair does."

Then she sits back on her heels and answers Tisk, with Ixx carrying it a piece at a time. "My hand, Old Tisk. Everything I've ever done with this light, I chose to do — the Lantern has never moved my fingers for me. If it wants a thing opened, or kept shut, it will have to tell me so plainly, and I'll still decide. But I'll be honest about the other half. I don't know what the debt is. If the thing you said is true — that what is lent, someone comes for — then I may be the one it's come to collect from, and I'd rather find that out standing at your door with my eyes open than have it find me asleep in the Gate."

She raises her voice a little for the next part. "That smell isn't a beast. Every book I've read that stood near dragonfire says smoke, musk, scorched meat. This is clean stone heated past what stone can bear — *called* fire. A bound elemental, a working on the Plane of Fire, something that eats heat. Its fumes are heavy; that's why they come in at the floor and the smoke-hole can't take them." She looks at Tisk. "Your deep door has a lantern that's been going out for a lifetime, and the stair gets warmer every year it fades. I think the lantern on that door is *holding* something, Tisk, and it's running out. And your Warm One has red scales and can't climb his own stair, and wants tall hands to open it."

Quietly: "So before I go down there I want to know two things. Does the Warm One know the flame is dying? And does he want the door opened to let something *out* — or to get *in* before the light goes?"

Tisk listens with her good eye on Sefa's face. At *I may be the one it's come to collect from*, the old kobold's head tilts a little to one side, the way a bird's does. When the whole answer is across, she lets out a long dry click.

"Tisk hears. Sefa's hand." Ixx straightens. "Sefa says the true thing and the bad thing together. Tisk likes this more than only the true thing."

She answers the questions one at a time. "Does he know the flame dies." Tisk lays a claw against her milky eye, then her good one. "He knows. He lies by the door. When the flame was big he did not look at it. Now he looks every sleep. Long."

The second takes her longer. She looks into the dung fire and clicks so softly that Ixx bends nearly to her snout. "Under the door is the fire-not-fire. It wants nothing. Fire only eats." Ixx swallows. "The Warm One is not under the door. The Warm One wants up." Tisk draws a line in the air with one claw, from the floor up toward the vault. "When Tisk already had eggs, the Warm One came down the stair. Small. Like a goat. Hungry. He followed the warm." She lowers the claw. "Down is easy." She stops there, and from the way her jaw sets she means to.

Sefa gets up slowly and lifts the beads so the mother can see she's taking the glow with her. "Keep the hide on them till the water cools. Then up to the bend, all the small ones, whenever the floor breathes like that. Pell — I think we go and hear him. But if he lies to me about that smell, I'll know it, and we walk back up."

Sefa doesn't need your flasks, so nobody takes one. Pikk's mother has her head out from under the hide now, though. Her eyes go to the flasks and rest there a moment, the way the little door-holder's went to the tools. Then she looks at you, sitting still against the wall, and her eyes stay on you a little longer than they did before.

Out in the dome the passage breathes in.

Claws on stone, fast. Skra comes through the gap in the hides at a run, her sling wound tight on her wrist, and a rasp from Hrask outside follows her in. Sefa's light falls on her heaving sides. She stops in the middle of the warren and speaks to the room, high and quick, and every kobold along the walls goes still again.

Ixx doesn't want to translate it. He looks at Tisk, and Tisk lifts her chin a finger's width.

"Skra brings his word," Ixx says. "The Warm One says…" His tail is around his ankle again. "'The tall ones talk long with the small ones. I am not small. The tall ones come now, or the tall ones go up, and the Broom goes with them.'" He stops. Skra rasps one more thing, and he adds, very quietly: "Skra says his breath was very hot when he said it."

Tisk doesn't move. Skra stands panting in the light and stares at the beads in Sefa's fingers, then at Sefa, waiting to see which of the tall ones answers.
````

Together after: [Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore]

## Round 11

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 11 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram hears Skra out, then meets it without raising his voice. He speaks so the whole warren hears him, not just Ixx.

"The Warm One wants us, he gets us. But we don't come down there running, with the Broom between us and his temper." He shifts his shield on his arm and steps back from the doorpost, just enough to gather his gear. "We'll go down in his time—now—but we go on our feet, not his summons."

He bends once, without hurrying, and takes up his sword belt and the javelins. He straps the longsword at his hip again, leaves it sheathed, and settles the javelin quiver across his back. The mail chimes once, low, and he's still careful about it.

Then he raises his chin toward Tisk.

"Old Tisk, we won't leave Pikk worse and we won't drag the Broom into a fight." He jerks his head toward the red passage. "Hrask and Skra brought us here; Ixx kept us alive on the stair. I'd have one of them at our back, not in front. Tell us what to step over, and we'll carry his answer ourselves."

He glances at Sefa and Pell.

"We go down now. Together. No one wanders from the path. Sefa, keep the light close; Pell, watch the walls and the floor-side shadows. Hrask, if you're coming, or any of your spear-kin, they'll be told we don't want their hides in front of him." He says it plain, then faces the passage.

"I'll take the red walk first. Let's see what's so old he can't climb his own stair."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa straightens up all the way. The beads stay lit in her fingers; she doesn't hurry, because every small face along the walls is watching to see whether the tall ones hurry.

"Skra." She says the name as carefully as Ixx said hers. "Carry this back, in his own words if you can. *The tall ones come now. Slow, talking, as they came down the stair. They were tending a sick child, and his door was not going anywhere.*" A beat. "And tell him I heard the rest. I'll take it up with him, not with the Broom."

She bends once more to the mother, touches the back of the small claw still clamped round a bead and gently works it free. "Keep him under the hide till the water's cool. Then the stair." Then to Tisk, lower, so it's only for the old one and Ixx: "Came down small as a goat, followed the warm, grew. And now he can't get back up his own stair and he wants *up*. I understand you, Old Tisk. Thank you for saying as much as you did." She presses her free palm flat to the dusty floor and holds it up, grey, the way Tisk did. "Dust hand. Whatever I do at the door, I'll do it with the Broom still living in this room. That I *can* promise, because it's mine."

Out through the hides, into the heat. She stops at the stair long enough to pick up her dagger and sheathe it, and to speak low while Bram buckles his belt and Pell gathers his blades.

"Here's what I've got. The smell is called fire, not dragonbreath — something bound or a working on the Plane of Fire, under the deep door. The lantern on that door is the same as mine, and it's been going out for Tisk's whole lifetime; the stair warms as it fades. The Warm One lies by the door watching the flame die. He came down this stair small, years ago, followed the heat, and grew. He *wants up*, and he's too big for his own stair now — so whatever he thinks is behind that door, he thinks it's a way out, or a way to become something that can leave." She looks at the red passage. "He's threatened to drive the kobolds out if we don't come. That tells me he needs us more than he wants us to know. We go and listen. Ixx walks first in the hot rooms — tread where he treads. Bram, light goes back on your shield, bright this time; I want to see the floor. Pell, keep your satchel open. And if it tells me the smell is its own breath, I'll know it's lying, and we'll have our answer about how much else it's told the truth of."

She turns to the doorway where Ixx stands half in, half out. "Ixx. Will you walk us down? If it's too much to ask, say so, and we'll follow Skra instead."

She lays two fingers on the shield-rim and the Light wakes there again, full this time. *(Light on Bram's shield; the beads go dark and back round my neck.)*

Pell Dunmore: I stand and shoulder the satchel. “I’m with you, Sefa. We hear him. Opening that door is a separate bargain.”

I give Tisk a small nod, then head to the curtain. “Ixx, will you still show us the safe footing?”

At the doorway I speak quietly to Bram. “He came down small and grew. The door holds something else—fire, with a failing light keeping it shut. He knows it’s failing and wants out. We need to hear how opening it helps.”

Outside the hides, I collect my weapons and tools, keeping every blade sheathed. I’m ready to follow behind Bram once Ixx leads.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Skra hears Bram out with her sides still heaving, then turns her snout to Ixx. He gives her Bram's words in clicks. It's quieter than when Bram said them, and he takes no longer over it than he has to.

Bram steps back from the doorpost and bends for his gear. He takes his time and the kobolds watch all of it. The sword belt goes round his hips and the longsword stays in its scabbard. The javelin quiver goes across his back, and his mail chimes once, low. Hrask watches the hilt go back on the hip. Then he looks at Bram's face. That's new.

Ixx carries what Bram says to Tisk. The old one hears it to the end and answers in a few slow clicks.

"Tisk says: Bram says *not worse*. Tisk hears." Ixx looks to Hrask, because the rest was meant for him.

Hrask answers Bram himself, in one long rasp, without looking at the passage. He keeps his spear upright, and when he's done he plants its butt back on the stone by the doorpost, where it was before.

"Hrask stays," Ixx says. "The Warm One says *the Broom goes with them*. So Hrask stands at the hides, by the small ones." He hesitates. "Hrask says the tall one's sword goes down the warm passage, not Hrask's spear. Is fair."

Then Sefa speaks to Skra. Ixx gives it over a piece at a time: *slow, talking… a sick child… his door was not going anywhere.* Skra's eyes go to the beads one last time. She clicks back what she's been told, quickly, to be sure she has it. Then she's gone through the gap in the hides and across the dark dome. Her claws fade off down the red passage.

Sefa works the bead out of Pikk's claw. He lets go with a small, cross wheeze, no cough. His mother pulls the hide back over both their heads.

Sefa holds up her grey palm, and Tisk looks at it for a long moment. Then the old kobold leans forward on her blackened stick and presses her own dusty palm against it, flat, scale to skin. She holds it there longer than a greeting takes. While Ixx passes on the promise, her claws find the shells at her throat.

"Tisk says: Sefa's hand, Sefa's promise. Tisk remembers both." Ixx listens to more, and his tail goes round his ankle. "Tisk says one thing for the hot rooms. In the hot rooms, little fires walk. Small, like Pikk's mother. They are not *his*. They do not talk. They know the Broom." Tisk clicks again, dry and short. "Tall ones, Tisk does not know if they know. Do not stop by the red cracks. Do not put iron in the red cracks."

She lets go of Sefa's palm, and by the look of her she's said what she meant to say.

Out in the dome Sefa takes her dagger off the bottom step and sheathes it. She gives the other two her reckoning in a low voice while Bram buckles up. Pell picks up his things one at a time: the shortsword, both daggers, the bow and the quiver, and the roll of oiled cloth goes back on his belt. He checks the cloth with his thumb as it goes. Nothing's missing. The small door-holder watches from the gap in the hides with its hands still behind its back.

Sefa lays two fingers on the inside of Bram's shield rim, and this time the Light wakes full. It's bright and lamp-yellow and throws the dome's curve back at them, the soot ring, the swept paths and the grey dust fan in front of the burned arch with its boot prints going in. The beads in her hand go dark, and she puts them back round her neck.

Ixx has his broom back. He comes out of the hides and stops in front of the three of them. He looks up at Bram, then at Sefa, then at Pell, the order they asked him in.

"Ixx walks." He doesn't say it gladly, but he doesn't take it back either. "Bram says kobolds not in front. Is good. Ixx does not want to be in front of him." He moves to Bram's left side, half a step behind his elbow, where the shield will be between him and the passage. "Ixx walks here. Bram walks first. In the hot rooms the broom says where. Bram's foot, Ixx's broom. Pell's foot, Bram's foot. Sefa last."

Bram faces the passage and says what he says about old things and stairs. He says it plainly, and the dome is quiet, so it carries. The breath coming up the passage stops for a moment. Then it comes out again at its old pace, a little warmer.

Ixx doesn't look at the passage, and he says nothing about it.

---

The red passage runs straight and slopes down, wide enough that the shield-light doesn't reach both walls at once. Within fifty feet it's hot enough that the mail on Bram's shoulders feels like a stove lid, and sweat runs down inside Pell's collar and stays there. The walls were dressed stone once. Along both sides, at waist height, runs a channel cut into the rock and lined with bronze. The metal has gone green-black, and every so often it's pierced with a row of little holes like a flute. Warm air whispers out of some of them. At one place the bronze has split along a seam, and the stone around the split is glazed and shiny, like the inside of a kiln.

The struck-flint smell gets stronger with every step, and now it clings to the backs of their throats.

After about two hundred feet the passage levels out and opens, and Ixx stops with his broom held across Bram's knees.

"Hot rooms," he says.

It's a long hall, perhaps eighty feet to the far end, with square pillars down the middle holding up a low flat roof. The floor is laid in big flagstones, each about a stride across. There are gaps between them a finger wide, and under the gaps is a red light, as if the whole floor had been laid over coals. Most of the cracks just glow. Some of them breathe. As the company watches, a line of cracks to the right of the pillars flushes bright orange and lets out a sigh of heat that blurs the air above it, with a scatter of sparks. Then it dims again. Bram counts it the way he counted the passage, and it isn't regular. The left side of the hall is quieter, and the stones there are paler and less scorched. The swept path runs that way, a line of clean stones zigzagging between darker ones. Near the middle of the hall, off the path, a patch of flagstones has been blackened in a long smear, as though something burned there and was dragged away. Two shallow grooves run from it back toward the passage.

At the far end of the hall is an arch, and the light beyond it is a deeper red, slow and steady, like the passage's breath made visible.

Something moves in the cracks on the right.

Pell and Bram both catch it at the same moment. A thin crack between two of the breathing stones brightens, and something stands up out of it. It's knee-high and shaped like a man, a small hunched figure of embers with light running in its seams. It has no face, only two brighter spots where a face would be. It stays there, upright in the heat-shimmer, about forty feet off, and the brighter spots stay turned toward the shield-light. Then it folds back down into the crack, the glow sinks with it, and the stones go dull.

Ixx's tail is round his ankle. He doesn't comment on the little fire. He sets the bristle end of his broom on the first clean stone of the swept path, just past Bram's left boot.

"Here," he says. "Then the broom says next."

*(Out of game: no dice were needed this round. Everything you three declared happens as written.)*

 *(Out of game: all four happened in the last turn as narrated, and none needed a die. They were open speech and plain movement with nothing uncertain in them:)*

1. Bram's words reached Hrask through Ixx. Hrask answered that he stays at the hides with the small ones: "the tall one's sword goes down the warm passage, not Hrask's spear".
2. Bram asked what to step over and wanted a kobold at the back. Tisk answered with her warning about the little fires and the red cracks. Ixx took the place half a step behind Bram's left elbow, behind the shield.
3. Bram led the way down the red passage with Ixx guiding from that spot, and he stopped at the edge of the hot rooms where Ixx's broom marks the first safe stone.
4. Ixx gave Skra Sefa's message a piece at a time. She repeated it back to make sure she had it, then ran down the red passage to carry it.
````

He *wants up*, and he's too big for his own stair now — so whatever he thinks is behind that door, he thinks it's a way out, or a way to become something that can leave." She looks at the red passage. "He's threatened to drive the kobolds out if we don't come. That tells me he needs us more than he wants us to know. We go and listen. Ixx walks first in the hot rooms — tread where he treads. Bram, light goes back on your shield, bright this time; I want to see the floor. Pell, keep your satchel open. And if it tells me the smell is its own breath, I'll know it's lying, and we'll have our answer about how much else it's told the truth of."

````text
Pell picks up his things one at a time: the shortsword, both daggers, the bow and the quiver. The roll of oiled cloth goes back on his belt, and he checks it with his thumb as it goes. Every blade stays sheathed. The small door-holder watches from the gap in the hides with its hands still behind its back.

Sefa turns to the doorway, where Ixx stands half in and half out. "Ixx. Will you walk us down? If it's too much to ask, say so, and we'll follow Skra instead."

She lays two fingers on the inside of your shield rim, and this time the Light wakes full. It's bright and lamp-yellow, and it throws the dome's curve back at you: the soot ring, the swept paths, and the grey dust fan in front of the burned arch with its boot prints going in. The beads in her hand go dark, and she puts them back round her neck.

Ixx has his broom back. He comes out of the hides and stops in front of the three of you. He looks up at you, then at Sefa, then at Pell, in the order you asked him.

"Ixx walks." He doesn't say it gladly, but he doesn't take it back either. "Bram says kobolds not in front. Is good. Ixx does not want to be in front of him." He moves to your left side, half a step behind your elbow, where the shield will be between him and the passage. "Ixx walks here. Bram walks first. In the hot rooms the broom says where. Bram's foot, Ixx's broom. Pell's foot, Bram's foot. Sefa last."

You face the passage and say your piece about old things and stairs. You say it plainly, and the dome is quiet, so it carries. The breath coming up the passage stops for a moment. Then it comes out again at its old pace, a little warmer.

Ixx doesn't look at the passage, and he says nothing about it.

---

The red passage runs straight and slopes down. It's wide enough that the shield-light doesn't reach both walls at once. Within fifty feet it's hot enough that the mail on your shoulders feels like a stove lid. The walls were dressed stone once. Along both sides, at waist height, runs a channel cut into the rock and lined with bronze. The metal has gone green-black, and every so often it's pierced with a row of little holes, like a flute. Warm air whispers out of some of them. At one place the bronze has split along a seam, and the stone around the split is glazed and shiny, like the inside of a kiln.

The struck-flint smell gets stronger with every step, and now it clings to the back of your throat.

After about two hundred feet the passage levels out and opens up, and Ixx stops with his broom held across your knees.

"Hot rooms," he says.

It's a long hall, perhaps eighty feet to the far end, with square pillars down the middle holding up a low flat roof. The floor is laid in big flagstones, each about a stride across. Between them are gaps a finger wide, and under the gaps is a red light, as if the whole floor had been laid over coals. Most of the cracks just glow. Some of them breathe. As you watch, a line of cracks to the right of the pillars flushes bright orange and lets out a sigh of heat that blurs the air above it, with a scatter of sparks. Then it dims again. You count it the way you counted the passage, and it isn't regular. The left side of the hall is quieter, and the stones there are paler and less scorched. The swept path runs that way, a line of clean stones zigzagging between darker ones. Near the middle of the hall, off the path, a patch of flagstones has been blackened in a long smear, as though something burned there and was dragged away. Two shallow grooves run from it back toward the passage.

At the far end of the hall is an arch, and the light beyond it is a deeper red, slow and steady, like the passage's breath made visible.

Something moves in the cracks on the right.

You and Pell both catch it at the same moment. A thin crack between two of the breathing stones brightens, and something stands up out of it. It's knee-high and shaped like a man, a small hunched figure of embers with light running in its seams. It has no face, only two brighter spots where a face would be. It stays there, upright in the heat-shimmer about forty feet off, with the brighter spots turned toward your shield-light. Then it folds back down into the crack. The glow sinks with it, and the stones go dull.

Ixx's tail is round his ankle. He says nothing about the little fire. He sets the bristle end of his broom on the first clean stone of the swept path, just past your left boot.

"Here," he says. "Then the broom says next."
````

````text
Bram speaks to Skra without raising his voice, so the whole warren hears him. "The Warm One wants us, he gets us. But we don't come down there running, with the Broom between us and his temper." He shifts his shield on his arm and steps back from the doorpost. "We'll go down in his time—now—but we go on our feet, not his summons." Then he raises his chin toward Tisk. "Old Tisk, we won't leave Pikk worse and we won't drag the Broom into a fight." He jerks his head toward the red passage. "Hrask and Skra brought us here; Ixx kept us alive on the stair. I'd have one of them at our back, not in front. Tell us what to step over, and we'll carry his answer ourselves." He glances at you and Pell. "We go down now. Together. No one wanders from the path. Sefa, keep the light close; Pell, watch the walls and the floor-side shadows. Hrask, if you're coming, or any of your spear-kin, they'll be told we don't want their hides in front of him."

Pell stands and shoulders his satchel. "I'm with you, Sefa. We hear him. Opening that door is a separate bargain." He gives Tisk a small nod and goes to the curtain. "Ixx, will you still show us the safe footing?" At the doorway he says something quietly to Bram.

Skra hears Bram out with her sides still heaving, then turns her snout to Ixx. He passes Bram's words on in clicks. He says them more quietly than Bram did and takes no longer over it than he has to.

Bram bends for his gear. He takes his time, and the kobolds watch all of it. The sword belt goes round his hips and the longsword stays in its scabbard. The javelin quiver goes across his back, and his mail chimes once, low. Hrask watches the hilt go back on his hip, then looks at Bram's face.

Ixx carries Bram's words to Tisk. The old one hears them to the end and answers in a few slow clicks.

"Tisk says: Bram says *not worse*. Tisk hears." Ixx looks to Hrask, because the rest was meant for him.

Ixx gives Skra your message a piece at a time: *slow, talking… a sick child… his door was not going anywhere.* Skra's eyes go to the beads one last time. She lets go of your palm. By the look of her, she has said what she meant to say.

Bram's foot, Ixx's broom. Pell's foot, Bram's foot. Sefa last."

Bram faces the passage. "I'll take the red walk first. Let's see what's so old he can't climb his own stair." He says it plainly, and the dome is quiet, so it carries. The breath coming up the passage stops for a moment. Then it comes out again at its old pace, a little warmer.
````
