**Sefa Marrin's screen (the relay):**

````text
You can't read it. A patient has a face, and this thing has two bright coals where a face would be. Its leaning could be wanting, or hunger, or simply what fire does near something that burns. With nothing behind the embers for you to read, you can't tell those apart. What you know is what anyone in the room can see. It came up ninety feet of stair after the light, it stopped where the light stops, and its reaching arm is still out over the step, a hand's width from the yellow.

The draught breathes up past it, warm and slow. Bram's shield doesn't move. The empty bedroll lies folded on the flag, and the plate burns a quiet yellow in your bare hand.
````

**Pell Dunmore's screen (the relay):**

````text
Bram doesn't shift his feet. His shield comes up two fingers' width and settles square to the mouth of the side stair, and he puts himself fully between the little fire and the room, the way he'd fill a gap in a wagon line.

"Nobody moves fast," he says, low and even. "It's watching the plate. That's what brought it up. It didn't climb all this way for our boots or our books. Pell, get a bedroll in your hands. If sparks jump toward the shelves, smother them. Sefa, stay where I can see your hands."

He turns his head just enough to speak behind him without losing sight of the stairs. "Lift the plate again—your hand, not skin. Bring it to the clear space between us and the steps. Slow. Don't wave it. Hold it out where the thing can look at it. If it follows the light, we learn something. If it backs off, better still."

Then, to the creature, calm: "Easy, little coal. We're not stealing your warmth. We're taking it back up the long way. We'll be clear of your house soon." He doesn't advance and doesn't lower the shield.

The little fire doesn't answer. Its crackle doesn't change. Its two bright spots never leave the yellow pool on the floor behind his boots, and as far as it's concerned he could be a door.

Sefa lifts one hand toward Bram, palm out. "Don't. Shield stays where it is, hand off the hilt. It's not looking at you." Her voice is low and level. "Tisk said they don't talk and they know the Broom. It's come up ninety feet of dead paper to the edge of this room and stopped at the cool air. If it wanted us it had the whole stair to decide. It's looking at *that*." She tips her head at the yellow pool on the flag.

She goes down on one knee beside the plate and closes her bare hand around its plain end. The barleycorn flame at the far end leans along the bronze toward the heel of her hand and stays leaning. Her palm doesn't redden. She lifts the plate without hurrying and stands with it cupped at chest height, lantern side out, the way you'd carry a lamp down a ward where people are sleeping.

The thing on the step rises as the plate does. It doesn't climb. It straightens, the hunch going out of its embers, and its spots follow the light up from the floor to the height of her chest. The crackle quickens, like green wood that has finally caught.

While her hands are busy, Sefa answers you. "Pell, your question, while I've both hands busy and can't fidget. *Candles not to light below.* The lintel out there says the same thing in older words: *no flame here but the lent one.* I don't think it was about paper. I think whoever briefed Harl knew that an open flame in this hill is a *call*. The house breathes called fire, the little fires are made of it, and a wick is a kin-voice to them. Your lantern's hooded and nothing came. The plate was uncovered and lit full for the first time in ten days, and here one is." She looks at the hunched shape of embers on the third step. "So no, we don't light a second lamp down here from anything but this. And we don't light anything at all near the cracks."

The bedroll you folded lies on the bare flag where the plate was lying, outside its light. It stays empty, because the plate is in Sefa's hand.

Then Sefa turns a quarter where she stands.

The pool of yellow swings across the room. It passes over the dead men's bedrolls, Bram's boots and the iron rim of his shield, and its edge comes to rest on the sill stone, one step above the little fire. That's as far as it reaches.

The thing leans toward it from the waist, the way a man leans toward a hearth on a cold night. Its seams brighten from red to orange. One arm of stacked embers comes up and reaches out over the edge of the second step, and stops a hand's width short of where the yellow lies on the stone. It holds there. It doesn't come up and it doesn't go back down. It sways, and the light runs in its seams.

Sefa watches it closely.

"Bram, back toward the slab door, slow, shield to it. You first this time. I'll come last with the light so it can see where the thing it wants is going. Pell, lantern shuttered to a slit. Keep the papers against your ribs. If it follows us into the library, it follows at our pace and we let it. If it goes back down the steps—" a breath, "then I think I've learned what it came for, and I'll have one more thing to tell Tisk tonight besides four names."

The thing came up ninety feet of stair after the light, it stopped where the light stops, and its reaching arm is still out over the step, a hand's width from the yellow.

The draught breathes up past it, warm and slow. Bram's shield doesn't move. Your empty bedroll lies folded on the flag, and the plate burns a quiet yellow in Sefa's bare hand.
````

Together after: [Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore]

## Round 25

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 25 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram nods once, slow, without taking his eyes off the fire.

"Right. Slow back. No metal, no flames—except the plate." He shifts his rear foot an inch, feeling for the floor behind him, but doesn't turn. "Pell, I need your hand on my shoulder through that gap. Don't guide me hard, just keep me straight. Sefa, call when you're moving so I know how far I've got to go. We go in your order: me, Pell, then you with the light."

He steps back by half a stride, shield braced. The little fire doesn't surge.

"Same as the stair. If it follows, we stay at its pace. If it starts to brighten or drop low, I want a shout, not a hero." He doesn't say what for. They know.

"They're not afraid of us," he adds quietly, "and that's just as well. It didn't flinch at the shield or the mail, but it won't take its eyes off the yellow. Sefa, you're the torchbearer now. Keep it at chest height and don't lower it. If it follows, let it follow. We'll take it as far as the clean stones outside and see if it goes home at the red cracks."

Over his shoulder, he says, "And nobody lights so much as a pipe till we're breathing surface air."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa lowers the plate a little, so the edge of its light draws back off the sill stone by a hand's width, and watches the reaching arm. Then she lets out a slow breath through her nose.

"I can't read it. No face, nothing behind the coals — it could be wanting, or hunger, or only what fire does near something warm. I won't guess with a dead wizard's library at our backs." Low, steady. "So we do the plain thing. We leave, the way we said, and we see whether it follows the light or goes home."

She shifts the plate to her left hand and cups her right over it, dimming the pool to a slit of yellow between her fingers — the way she carried the beads for Pikk, so the light shows the floor and not the room.

"Bram, through the slab door first, shield facing it, slow. Pell, lantern to a slit, you next; leave the bedroll — it's covered, and I'd rather have your hands free under that beam than full of blanket. I come last. If it climbs the last three steps while we're going, say so, don't swing. If it reaches into the light, I'll pull the light back, as I just did, and we'll see whether it can bear to stop." She glances at the hunched embers one more time. "If it comes with us through the library at our pace, it comes. It knew to stop at cool air; it may know to stop at the dome. If it goes back down to the house, then I think what's under that lid knows this light well enough to miss it, and that's a thing I'll want to sit with before I give anyone an answer."

She waits for Bram's mail to clear the gap, then Pell's shoulder, and only then turns side-on and backs toward the slab door herself, the plate cupped against her, the Book warm at her hip, keeping the slit of yellow pointed at the floor between her boots and the third step.

Under the fallen beam she goes through bent low, the plate held out ahead of her so its light runs along the stone and not the char overhead, and she doesn't breathe until the ash stops sifting.

Pell Dunmore: I pick up my bedroll and shutter the lantern to a slit.

“Sefa, I won’t strike it. But if it follows, let’s stop here before leading living embers under burned wooden shelves. That beam barely held while we passed.”

I move to the library doorway, keeping clear for Bram to pass through, and watch the little fire’s feet as Sefa moves the plate away. I keep the folded bedroll ready to smother stray sparks without approaching the creature.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram eases his back foot across the flag and tests the floor behind him before he puts weight on it. Then he takes the half stride. His shield stays square to the stair. On the third step the little fire doesn't surge and doesn't sink. It sways where it is, and its two bright spots stay on the yellow.

Pell picks his folded bedroll up off the flag, tucks it under his arm and turns his lantern shutter down to a slit. He goes to the slab door and puts his free hand flat on Bram's shoulder. Bram backs into the gap side-on, shield toward the room, and his mail scrapes both jambs. Pell's palm keeps him straight without pushing. Then Bram is through and standing in the dark of the library aisle, looking back through a gap a forearm wide at a room lit yellow and red. Pell stays at the jamb.

Sefa says her piece about not guessing with a library at their backs. Then she draws the plate back, and the edge of the light slides off the sill stone by a hand's width.

The reaching arm comes after it. There's no lunge in it. It moves the way a man's hand follows a warm cup across a table. The thing's foot comes up onto the second step, then onto the first, and it stops with its embers a hand's width from the yellow, as close as it was before, only higher.

She moves the plate to her left hand and cups her right hand over it. The pool shrinks to a slit of yellow on the stone between her boots. The rest of the room drops to Pell's slit of lantern light and the red glow on the stair.

The little fire comes up onto the sill.

It crosses from the warm stair air into the dead air of the room, and the change shows on it. Its crackle drops to the sound of damp wood. Its seams dim from orange to red, and it hunches lower than before. It keeps coming anyway. Each time Sefa takes a step back it takes one forward, and it stays a stride outside the edge of the yellow on the floor. It takes the route she took, round the edge of the dead men's camp and not through it, and its bright spots never leave her cupped hands. Through the gap Bram watches its glow climb the far jamb, then slide along the desks as it comes.

Pell is watching its feet, as he said he would, and he sees what the others miss. As it passes the end of the nearer bedroll, a spark comes off its shoulder like a flake off a struck coal. The spark drops and lands on the turned-back edge of the dead man's blanket. For a moment nothing happens. Then a thread of grey smoke rises from the wool, thin as a hair, and a smell of scorched wool drifts to the door.

The thing doesn't look back at it.

Sefa's heel touches the foot of the jamb. Behind her are the gap, Pell's shoulder and then Bram, with ninety feet of shelves full of ash-soft paper beyond them. In front of her are two strides of bare floor and the hunched embers at the edge of her slit of light. Past those, by the dead men's camp, the thread of smoke climbs straight up in the still air. The Book is warm at her hip.

*(Out of game: it followed, so Pell's "stop here" now stands. Sefa is at the slab door with her back to the gap, and whether she goes on under the beam is up to her player.)*

 *(Out of game: none of these four needed a die. All four were shown in the turn as you declared them.)*

1. **Bram's half stride back.** It's careful, unopposed movement on a clear floor, so there was nothing uncertain to roll. He took it and then backed through the gap with Pell's hand on his shoulder.
2. **Bram's call for shouts.** These are spoken orders, so no roll. The fire did dim and hunch lower once it reached the dead air, but nobody's paragraph had them shout, so I left the shout unplayed. Whether anyone calls it now is up to those players.
3. **Sefa lowering the plate.** A plain action. The light's edge slid off the sill, and the fire followed it up two steps.
4. **Sefa cupping the light.** A plain action. The pool shrank to a slit between her boots, and the fire came up onto the sill and into the room.
````
